C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

RSV 二价预融合 F 蛋白 疫苗 在妊娠期和预防婴儿 RSV 相关疾病中的作用

RSV Bivalent Prefusion F Protein Vaccine in Pregnancy and Protection Against RSV-Associated Illness in Infants
作者： Rabab Batool, Ran Wang, Hala Obeid, Tobias R. Kollmann, Joanne M. Langley, Pascal M. Lavoie, Bahaa Abu-Raya
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述的重点是妊娠期间施用 RSV 二价预融合 F 蛋白 疫苗 (RSVpreF) 在预防婴儿 RSV 相关下呼吸道感染 (LRTI) 方面的有效性。目标病原体是 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV),特别是 RSV-A 和 RSV-B。核心问题是妊娠期间 RSVpreF 疫苗接种 是否可以预防婴儿 RSV 相关疾病。
证据基础与方法
该综述是系统综述和荟萃分析。数据来源包括 PubMed、Scopus 和 Europe PMC,检索时间为 2020 年 1 月 1 日至 2026 年 1 月 30 日。初步确定了总共 1144 项研究,所有研究均由 2 名评审员独立筛选。主要结果是 RSV 相关的 LRTI 住院治疗,并使用 Hartung-Knapp 调整和 Sidik-Jonkman 估计量的随机效应荟萃分析汇总优势比 (OR)。有限的可用数据无法对急诊科就诊、重症监护病房入院和死亡等次要结果进行荟萃分析。
共识与结论
汇总的疫苗对与呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)相关的下呼吸道感染(LRTI)住院的预防效果在出生至3个月的婴儿中为82%(95%置信区间,81%-82%),在出生至6个月的婴儿中为78%(95%置信区间,70%-84%),当母亲在分娩前14天或更长时间接种疫苗时。这些结果表明,实施后的数据支持RSVpreF在妊娠期间预防婴儿出生后前6个月与RSV相关的下呼吸道感染住院的有效性。
争议与缺口
审查指出,可用数据有限,无法对次要结局(例如 RSV 相关急诊就诊、重症监护病房入院和死亡)的有效性进行荟萃分析。这表明关于 RSVpreF 对婴儿各种 RSV 相关健康结果的更广泛影响的证据存在差距。
科研与实践启示
结果支持将 RSVpreF 引入常规产前免疫计划。需要进一步的研究来评估 疫苗 对次要结果的有效性,并通过更全面的数据扩展证据基础。

RSV Bivalent Prefusion F Protein Vaccine in Pregnancy and Protection Against RSV-Associated Illness in Infants

Authors: Rabab Batool, Ran Wang, Hala Obeid, Tobias R. Kollmann, Joanne M. Langley, Pascal M. Lavoie, Bahaa Abu-Raya
Original abstract (English)
Importance The respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) bivalent prefusion F protein vaccine (RSVpreF) administered during pregnancy has demonstrated efficacy in protecting infants against early-life RSV disease in the pivotal clinical trial. Objective To conduct a systematic review and meta-analysis to synthesize real-world evidence on the effectiveness of RSVpreF in preventing RSV-associated lower respiratory tract infection (LRTI) in infants. Data Sources PubMed, Scopus, and Europe PMC were searched from January 1, 2020, through January 30, 2026. Study Selection A total of 1144 studies were initially identified. Eligible were nonrandomized studies reporting the effectiveness of RSVpreF vaccine in pregnancy in the prevention of RSV-associated LRTI in infants. Data Extraction and Synthesis All studies were screened independently by 2 reviewers. Data were extracted on RSV-associated outcomes among infants born to women who received RSVpreF vaccination during pregnancy and infants born to unvaccinated women. Main Outcomes and Measures The primary outcome was RSV-associated LRTI hospitalization. Odds ratios (ORs) were pooled using random-effects meta-analysis with the Hartung-Knapp adjustment and Sidik-Jonkman estimator. Vaccine effectiveness against RSV-associated LRTI hospitalization was calculated as (1 − OR) × 100% and assessed in infants from birth to age 3 months and from birth to 6 months, separately. The limited available data precluded meta-analysis of effectiveness against the secondary outcomes of RSV-associated emergency department visits, intensive care unit admissions, and death. Results A total of 7 studies were included, comprising 7112 infants, of whom 2684 were born to vaccinated mothers. The studies collected data from January 2024 through April 2025 from Argentina, the UK, and the US. Pooled vaccine effectiveness against RSV-associated LRTI hospitalization was 82% (95% CI, 81%-82%; 3 studies, 4397 participants; I 2 = 0%) in infants from birth to 3 months and 78% (95% CI, 70%-84%; 6 studies, 3008 participants; I 2 = 18.7%) in infants from birth to 6 months, when mothers were vaccinated 14 days or more before delivery. Conclusions and Relevance The results of this systematic review and meta-analysis suggest that postimplementation data support the effectiveness of RSVpreF in pregnancy for the prevention of RSV-associated LRTI hospitalization during the first 6 months of life. The results support the introduction of RSVpreF into routine antenatal immunization programs.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the effectiveness of the RSV bivalent prefusion F protein vaccine (RSVpreF) administered during pregnancy in preventing RSV-associated lower respiratory tract infection (LRTI) in infants. The target pathogen is respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), specifically RSV-A and RSV-B. The central question is whether RSVpreF vaccination during pregnancy provides protection against RSV-related illness in infants.
Evidence base and method
The review is a systematic review and meta-analysis. Data sources included PubMed, Scopus, and Europe PMC, searched from January 1, 2020, through January 30, 2026. A total of 1144 studies were initially identified, and all were screened independently by 2 reviewers. The primary outcome was RSV-associated LRTI hospitalization, and odds ratios (ORs) were pooled using random-effects meta-analysis with the Hartung-Knapp adjustment and Sidik-Jonkman estimator. The limited available data precluded meta-analysis of secondary outcomes such as emergency department visits, intensive care unit admissions, and death.
Consensus and conclusions
The pooled vaccine effectiveness against RSV-associated LRTI hospitalization was 82% (95% CI, 81%-82%) in infants from birth to 3 months and 78% (95% CI, 70%-84%) in infants from birth to 6 months, when mothers were vaccinated 14 days or more before delivery. These results suggest that postimplementation data support the effectiveness of RSVpreF in pregnancy for the prevention of RSV-associated LRTI hospitalization during the first 6 months of life.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that the limited available data precluded meta-analysis of effectiveness against secondary outcomes such as RSV-associated emergency department visits, intensive care unit admissions, and death. This indicates a gap in the evidence regarding the broader impact of RSVpreF on various RSV-related health outcomes in infants.
Research and practice implications
The results support the introduction of RSVpreF into routine antenatal immunization programs. Further research is needed to evaluate the vaccine's effectiveness against secondary outcomes and to expand the evidence base with more comprehensive data.

儿童急性呼吸道感染:意大利初级医院综合护理的叙述视角

Acute Respiratory Infections in Children: A Narrative Perspective on Integrated Primary–Hospital Care in Italy
作者： Cristiana Indolfi, Angela Klain, Giulio Dinardo, Carolina Grella, Salvatore Cascone, Michele Miraglia del Giudice
摘要中文翻译急性呼吸道感染 (ARIs) 仍然是全世界儿科咨询、急诊就诊和入院的主要原因之一,给医疗保健系统带来了巨大的临床、组织和经济负担。尽管大多数急性呼吸道感染是自限性病毒性疾病,但儿童时期的高 发病率 继续对初级保健儿科医生和医院服务构成挑战。近年来,随着 COVID-19 大流行和针对 RSV 的 尼塞韦单抗 免疫接种的推出,流行病学 和儿科呼吸道感染的预防有了很大发展,而 RespiVirNet 则加强了国家呼吸道病毒 监测。这些发展强化了对综合护理模式的需求。该叙述视角讨论了当前的 流行病学 和儿科 ARI 的负担(主要在意大利医疗保健环境中),并探讨了家庭和医院儿科医生在临床评估、风险分层、适当转诊和护理连续性方面的互补作用。特别关注抗菌药物管理和减少不当抗生素处方的策略。预防策略,包括 RSV 预防和流感、肺炎球菌和百日咳 疫苗接种,也被认为是协调儿科呼吸护理的组成部分。最后,我们讨论 监测、预防性干预措施、共享转诊标准、结构化信息传输和可衡量的质量指标如何支持更加协调的初级医院护理,并为前瞻性评估提供框架。

证据基础与方法
尽管大多数急性呼吸道感染是自限性病毒性疾病,但儿童时期的高 发病率 继续对初级保健儿科医生和医院服务构成挑战。

Acute Respiratory Infections in Children: A Narrative Perspective on Integrated Primary–Hospital Care in Italy

Authors: Cristiana Indolfi, Angela Klain, Giulio Dinardo, Carolina Grella, Salvatore Cascone, Michele Miraglia del Giudice
Original abstract (English)
Acute respiratory infections (ARIs) remain one of the leading causes of pediatric consultations, emergency department visits, and hospital admissions worldwide, imposing a substantial clinical, organizational, and economic burden on healthcare systems. Although most ARIs are self-limiting viral illnesses, their high incidence during childhood continues to challenge both primary care pediatricians and hospital services. In recent years, the epidemiology and prevention of pediatric respiratory infections have evolved considerably following the COVID-19 pandemic and the introduction of nirsevimab immunization against RSV, while RespiVirNet has strengthened national respiratory-virus surveillance. These developments reinforce the need for integrated models of care. This narrative perspective discusses the current epidemiology and burden of pediatric ARIs, predominantly within the Italian healthcare setting, and examines the complementary roles of family and hospital pediatricians in clinical assessment, risk stratification, appropriate referral, and continuity of care. Particular attention is paid to antimicrobial stewardship and strategies to reduce inappropriate antibiotic prescribing. Preventive strategies, including RSV prevention and influenza, pneumococcal, and pertussis vaccination, are also considered as integral components of coordinated pediatric respiratory care. Finally, we discuss how surveillance, preventive interventions, shared referral criteria, structured information transfer, and measurable quality indicators could support more coordinated primary–hospital care and provide a framework for prospective evaluation.

Scope and question
Acute respiratory infections (ARIs) remain one of the leading causes of pediatric consultations, emergency department visits, and hospital admissions worldwide, imposing a substantial clinical, organizational, and economic burden on healthcare systems.
Evidence base and method
Although most ARIs are self-limiting viral illnesses, their high incidence during childhood continues to challenge both primary care pediatricians and hospital services.
Consensus and conclusions
In recent years, the epidemiology and prevention of pediatric respiratory infections have evolved considerably following the COVID-19 pandemic and the introduction of nirsevimab immunization against RSV, while RespiVirNet has strengthened national respiratory-virus surveillance.
Controversies and gaps
These developments reinforce the need for integrated models of care.
Research and practice implications
This narrative perspective discusses the current epidemiology and burden of pediatric ARIs, predominantly within the Italian healthcare setting, and examines the complementary roles of family and hospital pediatricians in clinical assessment, risk stratification, appropriate referral, and continuity of care.

RNA病毒感染中的犬尿氨酸途径:抗病毒药物防御和病毒持久性的免疫代谢控制

The kynurenine pathway in RNA virus infection: immunometabolic control of antiviral defense and viral persistence
作者： Daniela Nahomi Calderón-Sandate, Manuel Josafat Huerta-Garza, Blanca Azucena Marquez-Reyna, David Mauricio Cañedo-Figueroa, Ximena Hernández-Rodríguez, Flor Itzel Lira-Hernández, Juan Fidel Osuna‐Ramos, Jaime Cardoso-Ortiz, Rosa María del Ángel, Alan Orlando Santos-Mena
摘要中文翻译RNA病毒感染是由病毒复制、先天免疫感应和宿主代谢之间的相互作用决定的,它们共同决定了宿主反应的幅度、持续时间和质量。色氨酸-犬尿氨酸 (Trp-Kyn) 通路已成为连接干扰素驱动的炎症、氨基酸可用性、免疫细胞功能、组织稳态和病毒持久性的重要免疫代谢轴。本综述探讨了吲哚胺 2,3-双加氧酶 1 (IDO1)、IDO2、色氨酸 2,3-双加氧酶 (TDO)、Trp 耗竭、一般控制非抑制性 2 (GCN2) 信号传导、雷帕霉素复合物 1 (mTORC1) 的机制靶点、Kyn 衍生代谢物和芳烃受体 (AhR) 激活在宿主 对 RNA 病毒感染的反应。我们整合了正义和负义单链 RNA 病毒、双链 RNA 病毒和反转录 RNA 病毒的遗传、药理学、细胞、动物和临床证据,包括 SARS-CoV-2、登革热病毒、寨卡病毒、丙型肝炎病毒、甲型流感病毒、呼吸道合胞病毒、轮状病毒、呼肠孤病毒、HIV-1 和 SIV。在这些系统中,Trp-Kyn-AhR 轴可以影响干扰素反应、病毒复制、免疫细胞功能、组织损伤和疾病结果。EMCV 心肌炎提供了因果原理证明,而其他 RNA 病毒模型则提供了补充的功能和临床证据。重要的是,通路激活并不赋予统一的 抗病毒药物 或致病表型;其生物学后果取决于病毒类别、组织向性、病毒负荷、炎症强度、细胞类型和疾病阶段。在此基础上,我们提出了一个时相模型,其中早期或过度的 IDO1-Kyn-AhR 信号传导可能会损害 I 型干扰素反应、NK 细胞功能、CD8+ T 细胞扩增和病毒清除,而在后期炎症或消退阶段适当调节激活可能会限制免疫病理学、支持上皮修复并保持组织稳态。相反,持续或失调的激活可能会导致疲劳样免疫状态、慢性炎症、神经免疫功能障碍和急性病毒后遗症。最后,我们讨论了阶段适应的治疗策略,包括 IDO1 抑制、AhR 激动或拮抗、KMO 定向调节、基于色氨酸的进入抑制剂和生物标志物引导的组合方法。总之,这些发现将 Trp-Kyn-AhR 轴定位为动态的、上下文相关的靶标,用于精确 宿主 定向的 抗病毒药物 免疫代谢。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注犬尿氨酸通路在 宿主 对 RNA 病毒感染的反应中的作用,特别研究了 Trp-Kyn-AhR 轴如何影响 抗病毒药物 防御、病毒持久性和疾病结果。核心问题是该免疫代谢轴如何与不同的 RNA 病毒(包括 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV))相互作用,形成 免疫应答 和 发病机制。
证据基础与方法
该综述整合了各种 RNA 病毒类型的遗传、药理学、细胞、动物和临床证据,包括正义和负义单链 RNA 病毒、双链 RNA 病毒和反转录 RNA 病毒。它包括 SARS-CoV-2、登革热病毒、寨卡病毒、丙型肝炎病毒、甲型流感病毒、呼吸道合胞病毒、轮状病毒、呼肠孤病毒、HIV-1 和 SIV 作为在此框架内研究的病毒示例。
共识与结论
Trp-Kyn-AhR 轴在塑造 抗病毒药物 反应、影响干扰素产生、病毒复制、免疫细胞功能、组织损伤和疾病结果方面发挥着动态作用。该评论强调,通路激活可能会产生有益和有害的影响,具体取决于感染期间激活的时间和背景。
争议与缺口
该评论指出,通路激活不会产生统一的 抗病毒药物 或致病效应,其后果取决于病毒类别、组织向性、病毒负荷、炎症强度、细胞类型和疾病阶段等因素。需要对这些因素如何调节该途径对 RSV 感染的影响进行更详细的研究。
科研与实践启示
该综述提出了阶段适应的治疗策略,例如 IDO1 抑制、AhR 激动或拮抗、KMO 定向调节、基于 Trp 的进入抑制剂和生物标志物引导的组合方法,以根据具体情况调节 Trp-Kyn-AhR 轴。可以探索这些策略来改善 RSV 感染的 抗病毒药物 结局。

The kynurenine pathway in RNA virus infection: immunometabolic control of antiviral defense and viral persistence

Authors: Daniela Nahomi Calderón-Sandate, Manuel Josafat Huerta-Garza, Blanca Azucena Marquez-Reyna, David Mauricio Cañedo-Figueroa, Ximena Hernández-Rodríguez, Flor Itzel Lira-Hernández, Juan Fidel Osuna‐Ramos, Jaime Cardoso-Ortiz, Rosa María del Ángel, Alan Orlando Santos-Mena
Original abstract (English)
RNA virus infections are shaped by the interplay among viral replication, innate immune sensing, and host metabolism, which together determine the magnitude, duration, and quality of antiviral responses. The tryptophan–kynurenine (Trp–Kyn) pathway has emerged as an important immunometabolic axis linking interferon-driven inflammation, amino acid availability, immune-cell function, tissue homeostasis, and viral persistence. This review examines the roles of indoleamine 2,3-dioxygenase 1 (IDO1), IDO2, tryptophan 2,3-dioxygenase (TDO), Trp depletion, general control nonderepressible 2 (GCN2) signaling, mechanistic target of rapamycin complex 1 (mTORC1), Kyn-derived metabolites, and aryl hydrocarbon receptor (AhR) activation in host responses to RNA virus infection. We integrate genetic, pharmacological, cellular, animal, and clinical evidence across positive-sense and negative-sense single-stranded RNA viruses, double-stranded RNA viruses, and reverse-transcribing RNA viruses, including SARS-CoV-2, dengue virus, Zika virus, hepatitis C virus, influenza A virus, respiratory syncytial virus, rotavirus, reovirus, HIV-1, and SIV. Across these systems, the Trp–Kyn–AhR axis can influence interferon responses, viral replication, immune-cell function, tissue injury, and disease outcome. EMCV myocarditis provides causal proof of principle, whereas other RNA-virus models provide complementary functional and clinical evidence. Importantly, pathway activation does not confer a uniform antiviral or pathogenic phenotype; its biological consequences depend on viral class, tissue tropism, viral burden, inflammatory intensity, cell type, and disease stage. On this basis, we propose a time-phase model in which early or excessive IDO1–Kyn–AhR signaling may impair type I interferon responses, NK-cell function, CD8+ T-cell expansion, and viral clearance, whereas appropriately regulated activation during later inflammatory or resolution phases may limit immunopathology, support epithelial repair, and preserve tissue homeostasis. Persistent or dysregulated activation may instead contribute to exhaustion-like immune states, chronic inflammation, neuroimmune dysfunction, and post-acute viral sequelae. Finally, we discuss phase-adapted therapeutic strategies, including IDO1 inhibition, AhR agonism or antagonism, KMO-directed modulation, Trp-based entry inhibitors, and biomarker-guided combination approaches. Together, these findings position the Trp–Kyn–AhR axis as a dynamic, context-dependent target for precision host-directed antiviral immunometabolism.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the role of the kynurenine pathway in host responses to RNA virus infection, specifically examining how the Trp–Kyn–AhR axis influences antiviral defense, viral persistence, and disease outcomes. The central question is how this immunometabolic axis interacts with different RNA viruses, including respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), to shape immune responses and pathogenesis.
Evidence base and method
The review integrates genetic, pharmacological, cellular, animal, and clinical evidence across various RNA virus types, including positive-sense and negative-sense single-stranded RNA viruses, double-stranded RNA viruses, and reverse-transcribing RNA viruses. It includes SARS-CoV-2, dengue virus, Zika virus, hepatitis C virus, influenza A virus, respiratory syncytial virus, rotavirus, reovirus, HIV-1, and SIV as examples of viruses studied within this framework.
Consensus and conclusions
The Trp–Kyn–AhR axis plays a dynamic role in shaping antiviral responses, influencing interferon production, viral replication, immune-cell function, tissue injury, and disease outcomes. The review highlights that pathway activation can have both beneficial and detrimental effects depending on the timing and context of its activation during infection.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that pathway activation does not produce a uniform antiviral or pathogenic effect, and its consequences depend on factors such as viral class, tissue tropism, viral burden, inflammatory intensity, cell type, and disease stage. There is a need for more detailed studies on how these factors modulate the pathway's impact on RSV infection.
Research and practice implications
The review proposes phase-adapted therapeutic strategies, such as IDO1 inhibition, AhR agonism or antagonism, KMO-directed modulation, Trp-based entry inhibitors, and biomarker-guided combination approaches, to modulate the Trp–Kyn–AhR axis in a context-specific manner. These strategies could be explored for improving antiviral outcomes in RSV infection.

慢性阻塞性肺病相关肺动脉高压中的气道和肠道菌群失调、感染加重和肺血管重塑:微生物机制、因果不确定性和治疗意义

Airway and Gut Dysbiosis, Infectious Exacerbations and Pulmonary Vascular Remodelling in COPD-Associated Pulmonary Hypertension: Microbial Mechanisms, Causal Uncertainty and Therapeutic Implications
作者： Nilufar Akhmedova, Gulomjon Kholov, Ulugbek Ochilov, Nodira Shonazarova, Otabek Yuldashev, Gulkhayo Olimova, Sabina Istamova, Sitora Mukhammadiyeva, Nozima Kenjayeva, Jahongir Sharipov
摘要中文翻译肺动脉高压并发慢性阻塞性肺疾病(COPD-PH)的预后比单独气流限制更差,但其发病机制被认为主要是由于肺泡缺氧影响肺循环。该模型没有完全解释两个观察结果。首先,慢性阻塞性肺病的大多数急性加重都是传染性的,并且存在稳定的频繁加重表型,这种表型不能完全由肺活量测定的严重程度来解释。其次,慢性阻塞性肺病患者的气道不是无菌的。它们是常驻微生物群落的家园,随着疾病的进展,微生物群落也会发生变化,肠道微生物群落也会经历与疾病相关的变化。本综述探讨微生物因素是否积极促进肺血管重塑或仅反映晚期疾病。考虑三种候选暴露:慢性气道定植、气道和肠道菌群失调以及反复感染恶化。然后,我们检查了不可分型流感嗜血杆菌、肺炎链球菌、卡他莫拉菌、铜绿假单胞菌、鼻病毒、流感病毒、呼吸道合胞病毒 和 SARS-CoV-2 的毒力机制和 宿主 识别途径。然后,我们追踪微生物识别与血管损伤之间的联系途径:Toll 样受体信号传导和 NF-κB 激活;白细胞介素-6、肿瘤坏死因子-α和白细胞介素-1β;内皮素-1 上调对抗一氧化氮耗竭;活性氧和缺氧诱导因子信号传导与缺氧肺血管收缩相结合;并且,沿着肠-肺轴,脂多糖易位、三甲胺N-氧化物和产生短链脂肪酸的类群的消耗。在治疗上,有证据和血管学原理表明可以采取干预措施来降低病情恶化频率。肺血管扩张剂具有合理的生理学原理,尽管其临床疗效经常受到通气-灌注不匹配恶化的限制。目前的证据支持生物学合理性,而不是已证实的因果关系:没有直接的纵向人体研究证实微生物改变或微生物暴露会导致 COPD-PH 的肺血管重塑。我们确定了能够直接检验这一假设的研究设计。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
肺动脉高压并发慢性阻塞性肺疾病(COPD-PH)的预后比单独气流限制更差,但其发病机制被认为主要是由于肺泡缺氧影响肺循环。该模型没有完全解释两个观察结果。
证据基础与方法
本综述探讨微生物因素是否积极促进肺血管重塑或仅反映晚期疾病。考虑三种候选暴露:慢性气道定植、气道和肠道菌群失调以及反复感染恶化。然后,我们检查了不可分型流感嗜血杆菌、肺炎链球菌、卡他莫拉菌、铜绿假单胞菌、鼻病毒、流感病毒、呼吸道合胞病毒 和 SARS-CoV-2 的毒力机制和 宿主 识别途径。
共识与结论
目前的证据支持生物学合理性,而不是已证实的因果关系:没有直接的纵向人体研究证实微生物改变或微生物暴露会导致 COPD-PH 的肺血管重塑。
争议与缺口
肺血管扩张剂具有合理的生理学原理,尽管其临床疗效经常受到通气-灌注不匹配恶化的限制。
科研与实践启示
在治疗上,有证据和血管学原理表明可以采取干预措施来降低病情恶化频率。我们确定了能够直接检验这一假设的研究设计。

Airway and Gut Dysbiosis, Infectious Exacerbations and Pulmonary Vascular Remodelling in COPD-Associated Pulmonary Hypertension: Microbial Mechanisms, Causal Uncertainty and Therapeutic Implications

Authors: Nilufar Akhmedova, Gulomjon Kholov, Ulugbek Ochilov, Nodira Shonazarova, Otabek Yuldashev, Gulkhayo Olimova, Sabina Istamova, Sitora Mukhammadiyeva, Nozima Kenjayeva, Jahongir Sharipov
Original abstract (English)
Pulmonary hypertension complicating chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD-PH) has a worse prognosis than airflow limitation alone but its pathogenesis is thought to be primarily due to alveolar hypoxia affecting pulmonary circulation. Two observations are not fully explained by this model. First, the majority of exacerbations of COPD are infectious and there is a stable frequent-exacerbator phenotype that is not fully accounted for by spirometric severity. Second, the airways of COPD are not sterile. They are home to a resident microbial community that changes as the disease progresses and the gut microbial community also undergoes disease-associated changes. This review examines whether microbial factors actively contribute to pulmonary vascular remodelling or merely reflect advanced disease. Three candidate exposures are considered: chronic airway colonisation, dysbiosis of the airway and gut communities and recurrent infectious exacerbation. We then examine the virulence mechanisms and host-recognition pathways of non-typeable Haemophilus influenzae, Streptococcus pneumoniae, Moraxella catarrhalis, Pseudomonas aeruginosa, rhinovirus, influenza viruses, respiratory syncytial virus and SARS-CoV-2. We then trace the pathways linking microbial recognition to vascular injury: Toll-like receptor signalling and NF-κB activation; interleukin-6, tumour necrosis factor-α and interleukin-1β; endothelin-1 upregulation against nitric oxide depletion; reactive oxygen species and hypoxia-inducible factor signalling converging with hypoxic pulmonary vasoconstriction; and, along the gut–lung axis, lipopolysaccharide translocation, trimethylamine N-oxide and depletion of short-chain fatty acid-producing taxa. Therapeutically, there is evidence and a vascular rationale for interventions that decrease exacerbation frequency. Pulmonary vasodilators have a sound physiological rationale, although their clinical efficacy is frequently limited by worsening ventilation–perfusion mismatch. Current evidence supports biological plausibility rather than demonstrated causality: no direct longitudinal human study has established that microbial alterations or microbial exposure cause pulmonary vascular remodelling in COPD-PH. We identify study designs capable of directly testing this hypothesis.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Pulmonary hypertension complicating chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD-PH) has a worse prognosis than airflow limitation alone but its pathogenesis is thought to be primarily due to alveolar hypoxia affecting pulmonary circulation. Two observations are not fully explained by this model.
Evidence base and method
This review examines whether microbial factors actively contribute to pulmonary vascular remodelling or merely reflect advanced disease. Three candidate exposures are considered: chronic airway colonisation, dysbiosis of the airway and gut communities and recurrent infectious exacerbation. We then examine the virulence mechanisms and host-recognition pathways of non-typeable Haemophilus influenzae, Streptococcus pneumoniae, Moraxella catarrhalis, Pseudomonas aeruginosa, rhinovirus, influenza viruses, respiratory syncytial virus and SARS-CoV-2.
Consensus and conclusions
Current evidence supports biological plausibility rather than demonstrated causality: no direct longitudinal human study has established that microbial alterations or microbial exposure cause pulmonary vascular remodelling in COPD-PH.
Controversies and gaps
Pulmonary vasodilators have a sound physiological rationale, although their clinical efficacy is frequently limited by worsening ventilation–perfusion mismatch.
Research and practice implications
Therapeutically, there is evidence and a vascular rationale for interventions that decrease exacerbation frequency. We identify study designs capable of directly testing this hypothesis.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 英格兰孕产妇 疫苗接种 覆盖范围:2026 年 2 月报告 - GOV.UK

Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) maternal vaccination coverage in England: February 2026 report - GOV.UK
新闻简报这份报告由英国健康安全局于 2026 年 7 月 2 日发布,详细介绍了截至 2026 年 2 月英国孕产妇 RSV 疫苗 覆盖率。报告指出,2026 年 2 月分娩的孕妇中有 62.5% 接种了 RSV 疫苗,但存在地区差异,例如西南地区报告的接种率最高,为 72.4%,伦敦最低,为 52.1%。综合护理委员会 (ICB) 也显示出显着差异,康沃尔郡和锡利群岛为 79.9%,伯明翰和索利哈尔为 40.1%。注意到基于种族的差异,各组之间存在 36.7 个百分点的差距。该数据基于改进的质量,涵盖自 2025 年 1 月以来超过 97.5% 的全科医生诊所。该计划旨在减少弱势群体(包括六个月以下的婴儿和老年人)与 RSV 相关的疾病、住院和死亡。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 7 月 2 日发布;活动日期为2026年2月;该报告涵盖截至 2026 年 2 月的数据。
地点与对象
英国,重点关注在 RSV 疫苗接种 计划第 18 个月分娩的孕妇,数据按 NHS 委托地区、综合护理委员会 (ICB) 和地方当局细分。
事件
英国健康安全局发布了一份关于英格兰孕产妇 RSV 疫苗接种 覆盖率的报告,详细介绍了 2026 年 2 月分娩的孕妇对 RSV 疫苗 的感染情况。该报告是官方统计发布。
规模、影响与风险
在 2026 年 2 月分娩的女性中,RSV 疫苗 的使用率为 62.5%(37,884 名中的 23,691 名)。不同地区的使用率差异很大,西南地区最高(72.4%),伦敦最低(52.1%)。不同的 ICB 的比例也有所不同,康沃尔郡和锡利群岛 (79.9%) 最高,伯明翰和索利哈尔 (40.1%) 最低。不同种族的接受情况各不相同,不同种族之间存在 36.7 个百分点的差距。
应对、状态与不确定性
它包括按地区、ICB 和种族划分的吸收情况分析,是国家免疫计划的一部分,旨在减少 6 个月以下婴儿和老年人与 RSV 相关的疾病、住院和死亡。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) maternal vaccination coverage in England: February 2026 report - GOV.UK

News Brief
This report, published by the UK Health Security Agency on 2 July 2026, details the maternal RSV vaccination coverage in England as of February 2026. It states that 62.5% of pregnant women who delivered in February 2026 received the RSV vaccine, with regional variations observed, such as the South West region reporting the highest uptake at 72.4% and London the lowest at 52.1%. Integrated care boards (ICBs) also showed significant differences, with Cornwall and the Isles of Scilly at 79.9% and Birmingham and Solihull at 40.1%. Ethnicity-based variations were noted, with a 36.7 percentage point gap between groups. The data is based on improved quality and covers over 97.5% of GP practices since January 2025. The programme aims to reduce RSV-related illness, hospitalisations, and deaths in vulnerable populations, including infants under six months and older adults.
Original source text (English)

This report includes vaccine uptake estimates for pregnant women who delivered in the 18th month of the maternal vaccination programme in February 2026. Main points The main messages of this report are: 62.5% (23,691 out of 37,884) of women that reported giving birth during the survey month received an RSV vaccine uptake varied by NHS commissioning region, with the highest uptake reported in the South West region (72.4%) and the lowest in the London region (52.1%) uptake varied across integrated care boards (ICBs) with the highest uptake reported in the Cornwall and the Isles of Scilly ICB (79.9%) and the lowest in Birmingham and Solihull (40.1%) uptake varied across local authorities with the highest uptake reported in the Isle of Wight (87.3%) and the lowest in Enfield (33.5%) uptake varied by ethnic group, with the highest uptake reported among the Asian or Asian British - Chinese ethnic group (76.8%) and the lowest among the Black - Caribbean ethnic group (40.1%) Help us improve this resource This vaccine uptake report has been designated as an Official Statistics release. Please complete the survey Your input will play an important role in shaping the development of this publication. Thank you for your time and support. Analysis of maternal RSV vaccine uptake Maternal uptake of the RSV vaccine is assessed monthly to monitor the reach and impact of the national immunisation programme. Given the programme’s design, uptake is defined as the proportion of pregnant women who delivered in the reporting month and had received the RSV vaccine during pregnancy. This metric serves as a proxy for vaccine coverage by infant birth month, reflecting the extent of passive immunity conferred to newborns. This monthly report presents RSV vaccine uptake for women who delivered in the eighteenth month of the RSV vaccination programme in February 2026. The monthly uptake trend among pregnant women who have delivered since the programme’s launch is presented in Figure 1. Overall, the monthly RSV maternal vaccine uptake for February 2026 was 62.5%. RSV uptake increased in the first few months of the programme and has remained steady since October 2025. Figure 1. RSV vaccine uptake (%) trend among pregnant women by month of delivery, measured in February 2026 Note 1: the vertical lines represent a change in the percentage of GP (general practitioner) practices used to calculate RSV vaccine uptake. Up to December 2024, vaccine uptake was estimated based on data from a single supplier covering fewer than 40.5% of GP practices. From January 2025 onwards, vaccine uptake was based on data from more than 97.5% of GP practices and is more representative of national vaccination uptake. This month’s report includes data from both major GP IT suppliers, significantly expanding the data available in earliest reports (September to December 2024). It includes data from 99% of GPs in England, up from 40.4% of participating GPs previously in the first 4 months (September to December 2024) of the vaccination programme. This has been made possible by improvements in data quality and permits a more representative analysis of uptake and trends across England. To further explore variation in uptake, Table 1 presents uptake by commissioning region, while Table 2 provides the breakdown by ethnicity. As shown in Table 1, there are notable regional differences in uptake, with variation of up to 20.3 percentage points between NHS commissioning regions. Table 1. This includes people whose ethnicity was not recorded, not stated, or who refused to give their ethnicity. The ‘any other ethnicity code’ category includes individuals whose ethnicity has been recorded using non-standard or legacy codes (that is, codes not aligned with the 2001 or 2011 Census classifications). The map in Figure 2 shows RSV uptake by ICB. Uptake varied across ICBs by 39.8 percentage points with the highest uptake reported in Cornwall and the Isles of Scilly (79.9) and the lowest uptake reported in Birmingham and Solihull (40.1). Figure 2. RSV vaccine uptake (%) among pregnant women delivering in February 2026, by ICBs Across the local authorities, uptake differed by 53.8 percentage points with the highest uptake reported in Isle of Wight (87.3%) and the lowest uptake reported in Enfield (33.5%). The variation across local authorities is presented in the map shown in Figure 3. Figure 3. RSV vaccine uptake (%) among pregnant women delivering in February 2026, by local authority Further detail, including uptake figures broken down by local authority and ICB, can be found in the February 2026 data tables. Since the RSV maternal vaccination programme has now been running for eighteen months (up to the reporting month), an increasing number of pregnant women are receiving the vaccine during their eligible window. This growing awareness and notification from gestation week 20 onwards have contributed to a gradual increase in monthly vaccine uptake from 40.5% in September 2024 to the current figure reported for February 2026. As the programme continues and more women are informed earlier in their pregnancies, monthly vaccination uptake is expected to gradually increase. Data sources and methodology More information is available in our quality and methodology information (QMI) report. The maternal RSV vaccination report will be released monthly, presenting vaccination uptake data only for the specific reporting month, without cumulative figures from previous months. The reporting period for the current report is from 1 February 2026 to 28 February 2026. This report focuses on pregnant women who delivered in the survey month, regardless of gestational age at birth. GP-level RSV vaccine uptake data is automatically uploaded via participating GP IT suppliers to the ImmForm website each month. ImmForm data is validated and analysed by the UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) to check data completeness, query any anomalous data and describe epidemiological trends. Since September 2024, the reporting data collected includes: denominator: number of women who delivered in the survey month, excluding miscarriages and stillbirths, regardless of gestational age numerator: number of women receiving RSV vaccination from week 28 of pregnancy up until delivery refusals: number of women in the denominator who refused RSV vaccination between week 28 of pregnancy and delivery To ensure accurate denominators are extracted from GP IT systems by the automated survey and precise uptake estimates are calculated, the methodology uses a monthly data extraction process with a three-month reporting lag to allow enough time for relevant information to be recorded. For example, data from September 2024 was extracted on 1 December, 2024. The data presented in this report for February 2026 is based on data received from both major GP IT suppliers, accounting for 99% of all national GP practices participating during the reporting month. The March 2026 maternal RSV vaccination uptake report is scheduled to be released on 6 August 2026. Background information The RSV vaccine has been offered to pregnant women in England since September 2024 to address the significant burden of RSV-related illness, hospitalisations and deaths, particularly among infants under six months of age and older adults, who are at increased risk of lower respiratory tract infection (LRTI) (see the Green Book on immunisation). In 2023, the Joint Committee on Vaccination and Immunisation (JCVI) issued a full statement recommending the implementation of a maternal RSV vaccination programme. This recommendation was based on robust evidence demonstrating the safety and effectiveness of RSV vaccines in protecting infants during their most vulnerable period. The vaccine is recommended as a year-round programme to be administered from 28 weeks of gestation onwards. This timing aligns with the optimal window for passive antibody transfer to the foetus, ensuring effective protection against severe RSV-related illness. From 1 September 2024 (the programme start date), all women who are at least 28 weeks pregnant are advised to receive the vaccine as soon as possible, with vaccination continuing to be recommended for all eligible women throughout pregnancy up until delivery. Additionally, the vaccine will be reoffered for every subsequent pregnancy to maintain consistent coverage and protection. This monthly report includes vaccine uptake data for women who delivered in February 2026. Programme delivery The programme is delivered through GP and maternity services, ensuring accessible vaccination opportunities for eligible pregnant women across England. In addition, the RSV vaccine is available through the community pharmacy delivery model at approximately 37 sites across 2 ICBs in the East of England. It is also available at up to 200 additional community pharmacy sites across the Midlands, North West, and London commissioning regions during the 2025 to 2026 expansion phase. Previous monthly reports are available at RSV maternal vaccination coverage in England. View five news elements
Time
Published 2 July 2026; event date is February 2026; the report covers data up to February 2026.
Location and population
England, focusing on pregnant women who delivered in the 18th month of the RSV vaccination programme, with data broken down by NHS commissioning regions, integrated care boards (ICBs), and local authorities.
Event
A report on the maternal RSV vaccination coverage in England, published by the UK Health Security Agency, detailing the uptake of the RSV vaccine among pregnant women who delivered in February 2026. The report is an official statistics release.
Scale, impact and risk
The RSV vaccine uptake was 62.5% (23,691 out of 37,884) among women who delivered in February 2026. Uptake varied significantly by region, with the highest in the South West (72.4%) and the lowest in London (52.1%). It also varied by ICB, with Cornwall and the Isles of Scilly (79.9%) having the highest and Birmingham and Solihull (40.1%) the lowest. Uptake varied by ethnicity, with a gap of 36.7 percentage points observed between ethnic groups.
Response, status and uncertainty
It includes analysis of uptake by region, ICB, and ethnicity, and is part of the national immunisation programme aimed at reducing RSV-related illness, hospitalisations, and deaths in infants under six months of age and older adults.

Respiratory Virus Activity Levels - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) published a report on September 4, 2026, highlighting respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) activity levels. The report includes general information about RSV and related health alerts, such as outbreaks linked to alfalfa sprouts and Salmonella. However, it does not confirm a specific RSV outbreak or provide detailed case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers. The CDC emphasizes the importance of monitoring RSV activity and outlines general prevention strategies, but no explicit investigation or containment measures are mentioned. The report is part of the CDC's broader efforts to provide public health updates and guidance on infectious diseases, including RSV, without specifying particular events or risks associated with the virus.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

呼吸道合胞病毒 - Britannica.com

respiratory syncytial virus - britannica.com
新闻简报The article from Britannica.com, published on September 6, 2026, provides general information about respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). It describes RSV as an infectious agent of the respiratory system that typically causes mild cold-like symptoms but can lead to severe lower respiratory disease in infants under six months and the elderly. The article notes that RSV infections are seasonal, occurring as annual epidemics during winter in temperate regions. It also mentions that nearly all children are infected by age two, with 60 to 70 percent infected by age one. The only available pharmacological treatment is ribavirin, which can be administered orally, parenterally, or by inhalation. Additionally, an injectable humanized monoclonal antibody is available for prevention in high-risk infants and children. Prevention measures include frequent handwashing, cleaning surfaces, avoiding close contact, and covering the mouth and nose when coughing or sneezing. The article does not report any specific outbreak or case count but outlines the general impact and response strategies for RSV.
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发布于2026-09-06。它讨论了RSV的季节性特征,通常在温带地区大多数国家的冬季以年度流行病形式出现(N11)
地点与对象
该文章是一般性的,没有指定特定地点或人群。它将 RSV 视为影响六个月以下婴儿和老年人的全球性问题(N7、N8)。
事件
本文提供了有关 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的一般信息,包括其作为呼吸系统传染源的作用及其与易感个体 (N7) 严重下呼吸道疾病的关联。
规模、影响与风险
An estimated 60 to 70 percent of children have been infected with RSV by age one, and nearly all children have been infected by age two (N9). RSV can cause severe bronchiolitis and pneumonia, particularly in infants and the elderly (N8).
应对、状态与不确定性

Britannica Editors Last updated Sep. 6, 2026 •History Britannica AI Ask Anything Table of Contents Table of Contents Ask Anything News • Medical groups offer vaccine guidance to fill federal vacuum • Sep. 2, 2026, 10:00 AM ET (NPR) ...(Show more) Hong Kong teen’s will to fight illness ‘incredibly low’ after near-death scares • Aug. 14, 2026, 4:12 AM ET (South China Morning Post) Show less respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), infectious agent of the respiratory system that typically produces a mild coldlike illness but in susceptible individuals, particularly infants under six months of age, is a major cause of severe and potentially fatal lower respiratory disease. Severe bronchiolitis and pneumonia caused by respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) occurs most commonly in the elderly and in young children. An estimated 60 to 70 percent of children have been infected with RSV by age one, and nearly all children have been infected by age two. Because RSV effectively evades immunity, repeat infections are common. RSV infection is seasonal, occurring as an annual epidemic during the winter in most countries in temperate regions. What's the Difference Between Bacteria and Viruses?Knowing the difference isn't just a technical distinction—it can save lives.(more) See all videos for this article Direct person-to-person contact is thought to be the most common route of RSV transmission. The virus also can be transmitted through the air, such as by coughing or sneezing, and through direct contact with a contaminated surface followed by touching of the face. Symptoms generally appear within four to six days of infection and include runny nose, cough, sore throat, headache, fever, and wheezing. In instances of severe illness, affected individuals may have a high fever, severe wheezing, rapid or laboured breathing, and bluish lips or fingers. Infants may only show signs of irritability, fatigue, and breathing difficulties, even in severe cases. The only pharmacological therapy available for treatment of RSV is the nucleoside analogue ribavirin, which can be administered orally, parenterally (by injection or infusion), or by inhalation. An injectable humanized monoclonal antibody is available for prevention of severe RSV illness in high-risk infants and children. It provides passive immunity and must by given by intramuscular injection once a month during RSV season. Transmission can be prevented with frequent handwashing, cleaning of surfaces, avoiding close contact with others, and covering the mouth and nose with the upper arm when coughing or sneezing. Britannica Editors History Britannica AI Ask Anything Quick Summary Table of Contents Table of Contents Quick Summary Ask Anything Top Questions What is a respiratory disease? Respiratory diseases are diseases and disorders that impact the airways and lungs, affecting human respiration. These diseases can affect any part of the respiratory system, including the nasal cavities, pharynx, larynx, trachea, bronchi, bronchioles, lung tissues, and respiratory muscles of the chest cage. The respiratory tract is susceptible to a wide range of disorders because it is exposed to the environment, has a large network of capillaries, and can be the site of allergic reactions. Common respiratory diseases in children include the common cold, tonsillitis, croup, allergic rhinitis, and asthma. 3 Britannica Sources childhood disease and disorder: Respiratory disorders respiratory disease therapeutics: The respiratory system This answer is created from Britannica articles using AI. AI can make mistakes, so verify using Britannica articles. Which parts of the body are affected by respiratory diseases? Respiratory diseases can affect any structure or organ involved in breathing. This includes the nasal cavities, pharynx (or throat), larynx, trachea (or windpipe), bronchi and bronchioles, the tissues of the lungs, and the respiratory muscles of the chest cage. Additionally, the pleura, the protective membrane covering the lungs, and the diaphragm can be affected by respiratory disease. 3 Britannica Sources infectious disease: Chlamydial organisms respiratory disease human respiratory system: The intrapulmonary conducting airways: bronchi and bronchioles This answer is created from Britannica articles using AI. AI can make mistakes, so verify using Britannica articles. What are some common types of respiratory diseases? Respiratory diseases affect the organs and structures involved in breathing, including the lungs, nasal cavities, and bronchi. Common types of respiratory diseases: * Infections Common infections include the common cold, influenza, pneumonia, bronchitis, tonsillitis, sinusitis, and croup. Lower respiratory tract infections are especially dangerous for young children, older people, and individuals with weakened immune systems. * Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease (COPD) COPD includes emphysema and chronic bronchitis, which damage the airways and reduce airflow in and out of the lungs. The leading cause of COPD is long-term exposure to tobacco smoke. * Allergic Disorders Asthma and allergic rhinitis are common allergic disorders that cause inflammation and obstruction of the respiratory tract. * Other Lung Conditions Other conditions include tuberculosis and cystic fibrosis, which predominantly affect the lungs but may involve other organs. 4 Britannica Sources List of Deadliest Diseases: Lower respiratory tract infection childhood disease and disorder: Respiratory disorders respiratory disease: Viral infections of the respiratory system therapeutics: The respiratory system This answer is created from Britannica articles using AI. AI can make mistakes, so verify using Britannica articles. How can respiratory diseases spread from one person to another? Respiratory diseases spread through various routes. Common methods include airborne transmission via respiratory droplets produced when an infected person coughs or sneezes. Close proximity to a contagious individual increases the risk of inhaling these droplets. Infection can also occur through direct contact with contaminated surfaces. If a person touches a surface with the virus and then touches their mouth, nose, or eyes, transmission may occur. Additionally, dust particles containing infectious agents can cause disease if they come into contact with food, skin, or are inhaled into the respiratory system. 6 Britannica Sources List of Deadliest Diseases: Lower respiratory tract infection infectious disease: The inanimate environment Questions About COVID-19 Answered: How is COVID-19 transmitted? Definition of respiratory syncytial virus infection: Transmission COVID-19 in 20 Questions: How does the disease spread? This answer is created from Britannica articles using AI. AI can make mistakes, so verify using Britannica articles. What symptoms might someone with a respiratory disease have? Symptoms of respiratory disease can include a cough, which may or may not produce sputum. The presence of blood in the sputum, known as hemoptysis, should always be checked by a medical professional. Another common symptom is dyspnea, or shortness of breath, which can arise suddenly or develop slowly over time. Chest pain may also occur, especially with pneumonia, and is typically felt when taking a deep breath. Wheezing in the chest may be heard due to narrowed airways. Other indicators include swelling of the fingertips, known as clubbing, and enlarged lymph nodes. General symptoms such as fatigue, malaise, loss of appetite, and weight loss can also indicate a respiratory illness. 3 Britannica Sources childhood disease and disorder: Respiratory disorders Synonyms of wheezing respiratory disease: Signs and symptoms This answer is created from Britannica articles using AI. AI can make mistakes, so verify using Britannica articles. How can people prevent or manage respiratory diseases? To prevent or manage respiratory diseases, minimize exposure to triggers, such as allergens and pollutants, and maintain good hygiene through frequent handwashing. Avoid smoking and exposure to secondhand smoke, as these are major risk factors for respiratory illnesses like COPD and lung cancer. For those with asthma, medications like corticosteroids, bronchodilators, and leukotriene receptor antagonists can help control symptoms and prevent episodes. Recognizing early warning signs and monitoring airflow obstruction with a peak flow meter are also important. Infectious respiratory diseases like RSV can be prevented by covering the mouth and nose when coughing or sneezing and cleaning surfaces. Vaccination and early treatment are crucial for managing diseases like tuberculosis. 4 Britannica Sources asthma: Treatment and management of asthma childhood disease and disorder: Respiratory disorders smoking: Lung disease respiratory syncytial virus This answer is created from Britannica articles using AI. AI can make mistakes, so verify using Britannica articles. Show more Show less respiratory disease, any of the diseases and disorders of the airways and the lungs that affect human respiration. Diseases of the respiratory system may affect any of the structures and organs that have to do with breathing, including the nasal cavities, the pharynx (or throat), the larynx, the trachea (or windpipe), the bronchi and bronchioles, the tissues of the lungs, and the respiratory muscles of the chest cage. emphysemaEmphysema destroys the walls of the alveoli of the lungs, resulting in a loss of surface area available for the exchange of oxygen and carbon dioxide during breathing. This produces symptoms of shortness of breath, coughing, and wheezing. In severe emphysema, difficulty in breathing leads to decreased oxygen intake, which causes headaches and symptoms of impaired mental ability.(more) The respiratory tract is the site of an exceptionally large range of disorders for three main reasons: (1) it is exposed to the environment and therefore may be affected by inhaled organisms, dusts, or gases; (2) it possesses a large network of capillaries through which the entire output of the heart has to pass, which means that diseases that affect the small blood vessels are likely to affect the lung; and (3) it may be the site of “sensitivity” or allergic phenomena that may profoundly affect function. Back to homepage By clicking "Sign Up", you consent to the Britannica Privacy Policy and Terms of Use. asthmaDuring normal breathing, inhaled air travels through two main channels (primary bronchi) that branch within each lung into smaller, narrower passages (bronchioles) and finally into the tiny, terminal bronchial tubes. During an asthma attack, smooth muscles that surround the airways spasm; this results in tightening of the airways, swelling and inflammation of the inner airway space (lumen) due to fluid buildup and infiltration by immune cells, and excessive secretion of mucus into the airways. Consequently, air is obstructed from circulating freely in the lungs and cannot be expired.(more) This article discusses the signs and symptoms of respiratory disease, the natural defenses of the human respiratory system, the methods of detecting respiratory disease, and the different diseases of the respiratory system. For more information about the anatomy of the human respiratory system and the process of respiration, see human respiratory system. Signs and symptoms Observe effects of smoking on lung tissue and examine various stages of respiratory diseaseLearn about tissue damage, in the forms of bronchitis and emphysema, that is evident when normal lung tissue is compared with lung tissue of light and heavy smokers.(more) See all videos for this article The symptoms of lung disease are relatively few. Cough is a particularly important sign of all diseases that affect any part of the bronchial tree. A cough productive of sputum is the most important manifestation of inflammatory or malignant diseases of the major airways, of which bronchitis is a common example. In severe bronchitis the mucous glands lining the bronchi enlarge greatly, and, commonly, 30 to 60 ml of sputum are produced in a 24-hour period, particularly in the first two hours after awakening in the morning. An irritative cough without sputum may be caused by extension of malignant disease to the bronchial tree from nearby organs. The presence of blood in the sputum (hemoptysis) is an important sign that should never be disregarded. Although it may result simply from an exacerbation of an existing infection, it may also indicate the presence of inflammation, capillary damage, or a tumour. Hemoptysis is also a classic sign of tuberculosis of the lungs. Britannica Quiz A Visit with the Word Doctor: Medical Vocabulary Quiz The second most important symptom of lung disease is dyspnea, or shortness of breath. This sensation, of complex origin, may arise acutely, as when a foreign body is inhaled into the trachea, or with the onset of a severe attack of asthma. More often, it is insidious in onset and slowly progressive. What is noted is a slowly progressive difficulty in completing some task, such as walking up a flight of stairs, playing golf, or walking uphill. The shortness of breath may vary in severity, but in diseases such as emphysema (see below Pulmonary emphysema), in which there is irreversible lung damage, it is constantly present. It may become so severe as to immobilize the victim, and tasks such as dressing cannot be performed without difficulty. Severe fibrosis of the lu...

Sanofi and Keke Palmer team up to urge parents to help protect babies from serious RSV with BEYFORTUS®
新闻简报赛诺菲和 Keke Palmer 发起了一项公共卫生运动,教育父母保护婴儿免受严重的 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 感染。该计划包括与这位女演员和企业家的合作,推广 BEYFORTUS® (尼塞韦单抗-alip) 的使用,这是一种单克隆抗体,被批准用于预防新生儿和 24 个月以下婴儿的严重 RSV 肺部感染。RSV 是一种高度传染性的病毒,可导致严重的呼吸道疾病,尤其是一岁以下的婴儿,并且是美国该年龄段住院的主要原因。该活动强调了在 RSV 季节之前或期间与婴儿医生讨论 RSV 预防的重要性,RSV 季节通常从秋季持续到春季。BEYFORTUS 提供长达五个月的快速保护,这是 RSV 季节的典型持续时间。该倡议是提高人们对 RSV 及其风险认识的更广泛努力的一部分,目的是鼓励采取积极措施保护婴儿。该活动不是 暴发 调查,而是预防性健康促进活动。

地点与对象
美国;1岁以下婴儿;婴儿进入第一个 RSV 季节;24 个月以下的儿童,在第二个季节仍面临严重 RSV 的风险。
事件
赛诺菲和 Keke Palmer 正在合作,教育家长如何使用 BEYFORTUS® 保护婴儿免受严重 RSV 肺部感染。该活动是一项公共卫生运动和营销活动,而不是 暴发 或 RSV 感染确诊病例。
规模、影响与风险
RSV 是美国一岁以下婴儿住院的主要原因。超过一半的婴儿会在一岁生日前感染 RSV。该活动鼓励父母与宝宝的医生讨论 RSV 预防问题,强调 RSV 季节期间免疫接种的重要性。
应对、状态与不确定性
该活动正在进行中,涉及公众教育和医疗咨询。BEYFORTUS 在广大婴儿群体中表现出一致的安全性。该响应不是 暴发 调查,而是预防性健康举措。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Sanofi and Keke Palmer team up to urge parents to help protect babies from serious RSV with BEYFORTUS®

News Brief
Sanofi and Keke Palmer have launched a public health campaign to educate parents about protecting their babies from serious respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) infections. The initiative, which includes a partnership with the actress and entrepreneur, promotes the use of BEYFORTUS® (nirsevimab-alip), a monoclonal antibody approved to prevent severe RSV lung infections in newborns and infants up to 24 months of age. RSV is a highly contagious virus that can lead to serious respiratory illness, particularly among infants under one year old, and is a leading cause of hospitalization in this age group in the United States. The campaign emphasizes the importance of discussing RSV prevention with a baby's doctor before or during the RSV season, which typically runs from fall through spring. BEYFORTUS provides rapid protection for five months, the typical duration of an RSV season. The initiative is part of broader efforts to raise awareness about RSV and its risks, with the goal of encouraging proactive measures to safeguard infants. The campaign is not an outbreak investigation but a preventive health promotion.

Sanofi and Keke Palmer team up to urge parents to help protect babies from serious RSV with BEYFORTUS® PR Newswire Tue, September 8, 2026 at 8:00 AM EDT 10 min read SNY Emmy Award-winning actress, entrepreneur and mom Keke Palmer partners with Sanofi to help educate families about protection against a serious respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) lung infection BEYFORTUS has been chosen by millions of parents to help protect their babies from serious RSV lung infection, a leading cause of hospitalization among babies under 1 year of age Campaign encourages parents to talk to their baby's doctor about helping protect their baby against serious RSV lung infection with BEYFORTUS, the #1 prescribed RSV immunization for babies MORRISTOWN, N.J., Sept. 8, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Sanofi is partnering with Emmy Award-winning actress, entrepreneur and mom Keke Palmer to help educate families about the importance of protection against serious RSV lung infection for babies. After she discussed the risks of RSV with her son's doctor, Palmer's baby received BEYFORTUS® (nirsevimab-alip), a monoclonal antibody approved to help prevent serious RSV lung infection in newborns and infants entering their first RSV season, as well as in children up to 24 months of age who remain at risk for severe RSV through their second season. "As moms, we spend so much time preparing for our baby's arrival and thinking about how to help keep them healthy. That includes being aware of RSV and the risk it can pose during a baby's first year of life," said Keke Palmer. "When I spoke with my son's pediatrician, we discussed options for serious RSV protection, and together we decided that BEYFORTUS was right for him. That's why I'm partnering with Sanofi to encourage parents to learn more about RSV and talk with their baby's doctor about a protection plan that works for their family. For my baby that included BEYFORTUS." Palmer's message comes as families prepare for RSV season. RSV is a common and highly contagious virus that can affect any baby. While it often causes mild, cold-like symptoms, it can sometimes lead to serious respiratory illness and remains a leading cause of hospitalization among infants younger than one year of age in the United States. More than half of babies will get RSV before their first birthday. For babies entering their first RSV season, which typically runs from fall through spring in most parts of the United States, planning ahead is important. Parents of older babies who are entering their first RSV season should talk to their baby's doctor now about RSV prevention so they can avoid missing their opportunity for immunization this season. Babies born during RSV season may receive BEYFORTUS at birth or at an early well-child visit. Story Continues "Our ambition is all infant protection, all season," said Ayman Chit, Head of North America Medical, Vaccines at Sanofi. "RSV is unpredictable, and it's difficult to know which babies may become seriously ill, that's why it's important for parents to have conversations with their baby's doctor about RSV protection before or during the RSV season. BEYFORTUS delivers ready-made antibodies, helping to provide rapid protection against serious RSV lung infection for 5 months, the typical length of an RSV season. More than 11 million parents in the US have chosen BEYFORTUS for their baby, and by partnering with Keke Palmer, we're encouraging more families to learn about RSV prevention and talk with their baby's doctor about BEYFORTUS." To learn more about BEYFORTUS and serious RSV lung infection prevention, visit BEYFORTUS.com. Parents are encouraged to talk with their baby's doctor to understand if BEYFORTUS is right for their child. Keke Palmer is a paid spokesperson for Sanofi. About RSV RSV is a highly contagious virus that can lead to serious respiratory illness for infants. More than half of babies will get RSV before their first birthday. Most of the time RSV will cause a mild, cold-like illness. However, RSV is the most common cause of lower respiratory tract disease (LRTD), including bronchiolitis and pneumonia, in infants. RSV LRTD is a leading cause of hospitalization in babies under 1, with most hospitalizations for RSV occurring in healthy infants born at term. Historically in the US, an estimated 590,000 RSV cases in babies under 1 have required medical care, including physician office, urgent care, emergency room visits and hospitalizations. About BEYFORTUS BEYFORTUS is a single-dose long-acting antibody designed to help prevent RSV LRTD in infants that extends through five months, the length of the typical RSV season in the US. For children undergoing cardiac surgery with cardiopulmonary bypass, an additional dose of BEYFORTUS is recommended as soon as the child is stable after surgery. An additional dose of BEYFORTUS is also indicated for children up to 24 months of age, regardless of body weight, who remain vulnerable to severe RSV disease through their second RSV season. As a long-acting antibody provided directly to newborns and infants as a single dose, BEYFORTUS offers fast-acting protection to help prevent lower respiratory tract disease caused by RSV without requiring activation of the immune system. It has demonstrated a consistent safety profile across a broad infant population. BEYFORTUS administration can be timed to coincide with the RSV season. BEYFORTUS has been approved for use in the US, the European Union, China, Japan, and many other countries around the world. Special designations to facilitate expedited development of BEYFORTUS were granted by several regulatory agencies, including Breakthrough Therapy Designation and Fast Track Designation from the US Food and Drug Administration; Breakthrough Therapy Designation and Priority Review designation by The China Center for Drug Evaluation under the National Medical Products Administration; access granted to the European Medicines Agency (EMA) Priority Medicines scheme and EMA accelerated assessment; Promising Innovative Medicine designation by the UK Medicines and Healthcare products Regulatory Agency; and BEYFORTUS has been named "a medicine for prioritized development" under the Project for Drug Selection to Promote New Drug Development in Pediatrics by the Japan Agency for Medical Research and Development. INDICATION BEYFORTUS is a prescription medicine used to help prevent a serious lung disease caused by Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) in: Newborns and babies under 1 year of age born during or entering their first RSV season. Children up to 24 months of age who remain at risk of severe RSV disease through their second RSV season. IMPORTANT SAFETY INFORMATION Your child should not take BEYFORTUS if your child has a history of serious allergic reactions to nirsevimab-alip or any of the ingredients in BEYFORTUS. Before your child receives BEYFORTUS, tell your healthcare provider about all of your child's medical conditions, including if your child: has ever had a reaction to BEYFORTUS. has bleeding or bruising problems. If your child has a problem with bleeding or bruises easily, an injection could cause a problem. Tell your healthcare provider about all the medicines your child takes, including prescription and over-the-counter medicines, vitamins, and herbal supplements. Your infant should not receive a medicine called palivizumab if they have already received BEYFORTUS in the same RSV season. Serious allergic reactions have happened with BEYFORTUS. Get medical help right away if your child has any of the following signs or symptoms of a serious allergic reaction: swelling of the face, mouth, or tongue difficulty swallowing or breathing unresponsiveness bluish color of skin, lips, or under fingernails muscle weakness severe rash, hives, or itching The most common side effects of BEYFORTUS include rash and pain, swelling, or hardness at the site of your child's injection. These are not all the possible side effects of BEYFORTUS. Call your healthcare provider if you have questions about side effects. Please see full Prescribing Information, including Patient Information, for more details. 医生们联合起来应对呼吸道病毒 疫苗 指南

Doctors Unite on Respiratory Virus Vaccine Guidance
新闻简报来自美国四个领先医师组织的医生发布了 2026-2027 年呼吸道病毒季节 疫苗 指南,涵盖流感、COVID-19 和 RSV。这些建议强调对婴儿、孕妇、老年人和免疫功能低下人群的保护。有证据表明,流感 疫苗接种 将 65 岁或以上成年人的住院风险降低了 31%,而孕产妇 RSV 疫苗接种 和婴儿接种 尼塞韦单抗 显着降低了住院风险。在 2025-2026 年季节期间,流感导致至少 390,000 人住院和 24,000 人死亡,RSV 与 170,000 至 340,000 人住院有关,而 COVID-19 与 140,000 至 250,000 人住院有关。该指南基于对 疫苗 安全性、有效性和 流行病学 的结构化审查,为临床医生在 疫苗 咨询和共同决策中使用提供了基于证据的框架。三项证据审查中没有发现新的安全问题。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该指南针对 2026-2027 年呼吸道病毒季节发布,证据审查涵盖 2025 年 8 月至 2026 年 6 月期间发表的研究。这些建议于 2026 年 9 月 4 日发布。
地点与对象
这些建议与美国相关,针对的人群包括婴儿、孕妇、老年人和免疫功能低下的个体。
事件
医师团体发布了 2026-2027 年呼吸道病毒季节 疫苗 指南,涵盖流感、COVID-19 和 RSV 预防。该指南基于对 疫苗 安全性、有效性和 流行病学 的结构化审查。
规模、影响与风险
流感在2025–2026赛季与至少390,000例住院和24,000例死亡有关。RSV导致了估计170,000到340,000例住院,而COVID-19则与140,000到250,000例住院相关。该指南强调了这些呼吸道病毒所带来的重大疾病负担。
应对、状态与不确定性
这些建议是由美国四个领先的医生组织发布的,三项证据审查中没有发现新的安全问题。该指南为 疫苗 咨询和共同决策提供了基于证据的框架。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Doctors Unite on Respiratory Virus Vaccine Guidance

News Brief
Doctors from four leading U.S. physician organizations have issued vaccine guidance for the 2026–2027 respiratory virus season, covering influenza, COVID-19, and RSV. The recommendations emphasize protection for infants, pregnant individuals, older adults, and immunocompromised populations. Evidence shows that influenza vaccination reduced hospitalization risk by 31% among adults aged 65 or older, while maternal RSV vaccination and infant immunization with nirsevimab significantly lowered hospitalization risks. During the 2025–2026 season, influenza caused at least 390,000 hospitalizations and 24,000 deaths, RSV was linked to 170,000 to 340,000 hospitalizations, and COVID-19 was associated with 140,000 to 250,000 hospitalizations. The guidance, based on structured reviews of vaccine safety, effectiveness, and epidemiology, provides an evidence-based framework for clinicians to use in vaccine counseling and shared decision making. No new safety concerns were identified across the three evidence reviews.

Evidence showed lower hospitalization and severe illness rates following vaccination. Recommendations cover influenza, COVID-19, and RSV prevention for the 2026–2027 season. NEW respiratory virus vaccine recommendations align physician groups on protection against influenza, COVID-19, and RSV. Respiratory Virus Vaccines Address a Substantial Disease Burden Four leading U.S. physician organizations have issued recommendations for the 2026–2027 respiratory virus season, covering patients from infancy through older adulthood, as well as pregnant and immunocompromised populations. The guidance followed structured reviews of vaccine safety, effectiveness, and epidemiology. Researchers examined 69 influenza studies, 155 COVID-19 studies, and 75 respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) studies published between August 2025 and June 2026. More than a dozen randomized controlled trials were included for each pathogen. The recommendations arrive as respiratory viruses continue to cause substantial morbidity and mortality. During the 2025–2026 season, influenza was associated with at least 390,000 hospitalizations and 24,000 deaths. RSV caused an estimated 170,000 to 340,000 hospitalizations, while COVID-19 was linked to between 140,000 and 250,000 hospitalizations. Evidence Supports Protection Against Severe Illness Influenza vaccination reduced hospitalization risk by 31% among adults aged 65 years or older. Vaccination during pregnancy reduced symptomatic influenza among infants younger than 6 months by 44%, while pediatric influenza vaccination was associated with an 80% reduction in deaths across eight seasons. COVID-19 vaccination reduced hospitalization risk by 53% among adults aged 65 years or older. Among people with immunocompromising conditions, vaccination lowered the risk of intensive care admission or death by 32% to 53%. Vaccination during pregnancy also reduced emergency department or urgent care visits by 58%. RSV vaccines were 69% to 83% effective against hospitalization among adults aged 60 years or older. Maternal RSV vaccination reduced RSV risk by 82%, while infant immunization with nirsevimab lowered hospitalization risk by 64% to 93%. No new safety concerns were identified across the three evidence reviews. Respiratory Virus Vaccine Recommendations by Population The guidance supports annual influenza vaccination for everyone aged 6 months or older. COVID-19 vaccination recommendations vary by age and clinical status, with particular emphasis on older adults, young children, pregnant patients, healthcare personnel, and people with compromised immune systems. RSV vaccination is recommended for adults aged 50 to 74 years with high risk conditions and all adults aged 75 years or older. Preventive options for infants include maternal RSV vaccination or nirsevimab, depending on eligibility and maternal vaccination history. For clinicians, the recommendations provide an evidence based framework for vaccine counseling and shared decision making ahead of the respiratory virus season. Reference O’Reilly KB. Doctors united on respiratory virus vaccine recommendations. 2 September 2026. Time
The guidance was issued for the 2026–2027 respiratory virus season, with evidence reviews covering studies published between August 2025 and June 2026. The recommendations were published on 4 September 2026.
Location and population
The recommendations are relevant to the United States, targeting populations including infants, pregnant individuals, older adults, and immunocompromised individuals.
Event
Physician groups issued vaccine guidance for the 2026–2027 respiratory virus season, covering influenza, COVID-19, and RSV prevention. The guidance was based on structured reviews of vaccine safety, effectiveness, and epidemiology.
Scale, impact and risk
Influenza was associated with at least 390,000 hospitalizations and 24,000 deaths during the 2025–2026 season. RSV caused an estimated 170,000 to 340,000 hospitalizations, while COVID-19 was linked to between 140,000 and 250,000 hospitalizations. The guidance highlights the substantial disease burden caused by these respiratory viruses.
Response, status and uncertainty
The recommendations were issued by four leading U.S. physician organizations, with no new safety concerns identified across the three evidence reviews. The guidance provides an evidence-based framework for vaccine counseling and shared decision making.

西海岸健康联盟致力于评估新证据并与社区分享更新的评估结果,这表明审查过程正在进行中。

Immunization recommendations aim to address the rising case numbers of COVID-19, influenza, RSV

News Brief
The West Coast Health Alliance has released updated immunization recommendations for the 2026-27 respiratory virus season, targeting RSV, influenza, and COVID-19. These recommendations, informed by guidance from national medical organizations, emphasize the importance of vaccines in reducing severe illness and healthcare strain. The advice includes specific groups such as children, pregnant individuals, and adults aged 65 and older. The recommendations are part of a broader public health strategy to address rising case numbers. The Alliance is committed to ongoing evaluation and updating of its guidance based on new evidence, ensuring communities receive the most current information.

Acording to West Coast Health Alliance, the recommendations are informed by guidance from trusted national medical organizations, including the American Academy of Pediatrics, the American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists, and the American Academy of Family Physicians. “As we enter the 2026-27 respiratory virus season, we recommend the recently available updated vaccine formulations to reduce the risk of severe illness,” said Hawai‘i State Epidemiologist Dr. Sarah Kemble. “We also continue to recommend other steps, such as practicing good hygiene and staying home when sick, to help people stay healthy, especially as we’re experiencing an increase in cases.” Immunization is one of the best protections available against serious illness caused by respiratory viruses such as COVID-19, influenza and respiratory syncytial virus. Seasonal immunization is also a critical public health tool to reduce serious illness and strain on health care systems. The West Coast Health Alliance believes that all recommended immunizations should be accessible to people in its member states and emphasizes the importance of protecting people most impacted by these respiratory viruses, especially those who are most vulnerable. The West Coast Health Alliance will continue to evaluate new evidence and recommendations as they become available and is committed to sharing updated assessments with communities. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

医疗团体提供秋季、冬季 疫苗 建议

Medical Groups Offer Vaccine Recommendations for Fall, Winter
新闻简报Medical groups in the United States are recommending vaccines against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), influenza, and COVID-19 for the fall and winter season. The American Academy of Pediatrics advises that children aged 6 to 23 months and those aged 2 to 18 in certain categories should receive RSV vaccines, while the CDC recommends annual flu vaccination for all people aged 6 months and older. However, the CDC has not updated its recommendations for influenza and COVID-19 vaccines this year due to legal uncertainties. A federal judge blocked an updated schedule for these vaccines in January 2026, citing insufficient procedural steps, and the CDC's vaccine advisers are unable to meet. The CDC's website states that its influenza vaccine recommendations are based on effectiveness data from late 2024 and early 2025, but the updated schedule remains unimplemented. The recommendations are made ahead of the virus season, which typically runs through the fall and winter. The CDC also recommends RSV vaccines for pregnant women, people 75 years of age and older, and those with specific health conditions. The American Academy of Family Physicians advises similar recommendations for children and adults. The CDC has not updated its page on COVID-19 vaccines this year, and the effectiveness of flu vaccines is estimated at 16 percent to 53 percent. Some critics, like Jane Orient, have raised concerns about the safety and effectiveness of RSV vaccines, noting that they are relatively new and lack sufficient data. The CDC's recommendations for RSV vaccines are not yet updated due to the ongoing legal challenges.
查看新闻五要素
时间
这些建议是在整个秋季和冬季的病毒季节之前提出的。CDC 的流感 疫苗 建议基于 2024 年底和 2025 年初的有效性数据。联邦法官的命令和持续上诉与 2026 年 1 月采取的行动有关。
地点与对象
这些建议适用于美国人,包括 6 至 23 个月的儿童、某些类别的 2 至 18 岁儿童、孕妇、75 岁及以上的人以及 50 至 74 岁患有某些疾病的人。CDC 还建议所有 6 个月及以上的人接种流感 疫苗。
事件
包括美国儿科学会和美国妇产科医师学会在内的医疗团体建议针对特定人群使用 疫苗,而不是 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV)。由于法律上的不确定性,CDC 尚未更新其针对流感和 COVID-19 疫苗 的建议。
规模、影响与风险
The recommendations cover a broad population, including children, pregnant women, and elderly individuals. The CDC's influenza vaccine recommendations are for annual vaccination for all people aged 6 months and older. The effectiveness of flu vaccines is estimated at 16 percent to 53 percent. The CDC has not updated its recommendations for COVID-19 vaccines this year.
应对、状态与不确定性
由于法律上的不确定性,CDC 针对流感 疫苗 的建议目前已到位。由于法院命令,CDC 的 疫苗 顾问小组无法召开会议。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Medical Groups Offer Vaccine Recommendations for Fall, Winter

News Brief
Medical groups in the United States are recommending vaccines against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), influenza, and COVID-19 for the fall and winter season. The American Academy of Pediatrics advises that children aged 6 to 23 months and those aged 2 to 18 in certain categories should receive RSV vaccines, while the CDC recommends annual flu vaccination for all people aged 6 months and older. However, the CDC has not updated its recommendations for influenza and COVID-19 vaccines this year due to legal uncertainties. The CDC's website states that its influenza vaccine recommendations are based on effectiveness data from late 2024 and early 2025, but the updated schedule remains unimplemented. The recommendations are made ahead of the virus season, which typically runs through the fall and winter. The CDC also recommends RSV vaccines for pregnant women, people 75 years of age and older, and those with specific health conditions. The American Academy of Family Physicians advises similar recommendations for children and adults. Original source text (English)
Open menu NTD Television U.S.ChinaWorldPoliticsHealthBusinessEntertainmentSports More ●LIVE search EN▼ NTD Television NewsHealth News Medical Groups Offer Vaccine Recommendations for Fall, Winter The groups advised many people should receive vaccines against influenza, COVID-19, and respiratory syncytial virus. By Zachary Stieber | Published: 9/3/2026, 4:01:55 PM EDT A nurse practitioner holds a vaccine in a file photograph. (Joe Raedle/Getty Images) Medical groups are recommending that many people receive vaccines against respiratory viruses this fall and winter. The American Academy of Pediatrics recommends COVID-19 vaccination for all children aged 6 to 23 months, as well as children aged 2 through 18 who fall in certain categories, such as being unvaccinated or living with people deemed at high risk for severe COVID-19. Other children aged 2 to 18 can receive a vaccine if their parents want them to, the academy said. The American Academy of Family Physicians is advising COVID-19 vaccinations for those same children, as well as virtually all adults. The groups are also recommending influenza vaccination for all people aged 6 months and older, and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) vaccination for pregnant women, people 75 years of age and older, and people aged 50 to 74 with certain conditions such as chronic liver disease or living in a nursing home. Also, children aged from zero to 19 months should generally receive RSV vaccination, according to the new advice, if their mother did not receive an RSV vaccine during pregnancy. The American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists is recommending pregnant women receive vaccines against COVID-19, flu, and RSV, as it has done before. The recommendations come ahead of the virus season, which runs through the fall and winter. The recommendations were based on effectiveness, including estimates that flu vaccines were 16 percent to 53 percent effective in late 2024 and early 2025. The organizations represent doctors and accept money or donations from vaccine manufacturers. The Department of Health and Human Services, the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention’s parent agency, did not respond to a request for comment by the time of publication. The CDC, with assistance from its vaccine advisory panel, usually updates vaccine recommendations regularly, including ahead of the virus season. In January, the CDC announced a new schedule that narrowed recommendations for COVID-19 and influenza vaccination, among others. A federal judge blocked the update, determining officials did not take proper steps before announcing it. The order has prevented the CDC’s vaccine advisers from meeting. An appeal is ongoing. The CDC website says that “because of legal uncertainties and inquiries,” its recommendations for influenza vaccines are for annual vaccination for all people aged 6 months and older, instead of the updated advice. Its page on COVID-19 vaccines has not been updated this year. Federal regulators just approved new COVID-19 vaccines without human data. The CDC would normally decide which people should receive the vaccines following new approvals, absent the court order. The CDC recommends pregnant women receive flu vaccines and RSV vaccines, but not COVID-19 vaccines. Dr. Jane Orient, executive director of the Association of American Physicians and Surgeons, which does not accept pharmaceutical funding, said that RSV vaccines are so new that there has not been enough time to assess them. Orient told The Epoch Times in an email that the influenza vaccine “is generally not very effective, and has a high incidence of adverse effects.” And she said that COVID-19 vaccines also have troubling adverse reactions, and that there are effective treatments for COVID-19, such as repurposed drugs. From The Epoch Times
View five news elements
Time
The recommendations are made ahead of the virus season, which runs through the fall and winter. The CDC's influenza vaccine recommendations were based on effectiveness data from late 2024 and early 2025. The federal judge's order and ongoing appeal relate to actions taken in January 2026.
Location and population
The recommendations are for people in the United States, including children aged 6 to 23 months, children aged 2 through 18 in certain categories, pregnant women, people 75 years of age and older, and people aged 50 to 74 with certain conditions. The CDC also recommends flu vaccines for all people aged 6 months and older.
Event
Medical groups, including the American Academy of Pediatrics and the American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists, are recommending vaccines against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) for specific populations. The CDC has not updated its recommendations for influenza and COVID-19 vaccines due to legal uncertainties.
Scale, impact and risk
The recommendations cover a broad population, including children, pregnant women, and elderly individuals. The CDC's influenza vaccine recommendations are for annual vaccination for all people aged 6 months and older. The effectiveness of flu vaccines is estimated at 16 percent to 53 percent. The CDC has not updated its recommendations for COVID-19 vaccines this year.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC's recommendations for influenza vaccines are currently in place due to legal uncertainties. The CDC's vaccine advisory panel is unable to meet due to a court order.
