B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

AAP 2026-2027 年 RSV 和 COVID-19 免疫指南 - Pharmacy Times

AAP Issue 2026-2027 Guidance on RSV and COVID-19 Immunization - Pharmacy Times
新闻简报美国儿科学会 (AAP) 发布了 2026-2027 季 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 和 COVID-19 免疫接种的更新指南。本指南概述了 疫苗 管理和预防策略的建议,特别是针对儿童和高危人群。该指南于 2026 年 9 月 3 日发布,基于最新的现有证据和专家共识。它强调了免疫接种在降低严重呼吸道疾病风险方面的重要性,这是一个重大的公共卫生问题。AAP 的指导是官方回应,并不表示任何未解决的不确定性或正在进行的调查。该指南是加强预防保健和管理这些病毒在脆弱社区传播的更广泛努力的一部分。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该指南于 2026 年发布,最新新闻文章发布于 2026 年 9 月 3 日,其他相关文章发布于 8 至 25 天之前。
地点与对象
该指南适用于美国一般人群,因其由美国儿科学会(AAP)发布,并聚焦于呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)和新冠状病毒(COVID-19)疫苗接种在儿童及高风险群体中的应用。
事件
AAP 发布了 2026-2027 年 RSV 和 COVID-19 免疫指南,其中包括 疫苗 管理和预防策略的建议。这是根据官方指导文件确认的事件。
规模、影响与风险
该指南涉及 RSV 和 COVID-19 的预防和管理,这是重大的公共卫生问题,特别是对于儿童和弱势群体而言。所提供的证据并未量化影响规模,但由于可能导致严重呼吸道疾病,因此明确指出风险较高。
应对、状态与不确定性
该指南目前已就位,代表 AAP 的官方回应。所提供的证据中没有迹象表明调查正在进行或未解决的不确定性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

AAP Issue 2026-2027 Guidance on RSV and COVID-19 Immunization - Pharmacy Times

News Brief
The American Academy of Pediatrics (AAP) has issued updated guidance for the 2026-2027 season on the immunization against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and COVID-19. This guidance outlines recommendations for vaccine administration and prevention strategies, particularly targeting children and at-risk populations. The guidance was published on September 3, 2026, and is based on the latest available evidence and expert consensus. It emphasizes the importance of immunization in reducing the risk of severe respiratory illness, which is a significant public health concern. The AAP's guidance is an official response and does not indicate any unresolved uncertainties or ongoing investigations. The guidance is part of a broader effort to enhance preventive care and manage the spread of these viruses in vulnerable communities.
Original source text (English)

新审查显示,挥之不去的呼吸道病毒症状不仅限于新冠病毒 - CIDRAP

Lingering respiratory virus symptoms aren’t limited to COVID, new review shows - CIDRAP
新闻简报CIDRAP 的一项新审查显示,持续存在的呼吸道病毒症状并非 COVID-19 所独有,在流感和 RSV 等其他呼吸道感染之后也很常见。该报告于 2026 年 9 月 8 日发布,强调需要更广泛地了解感染后的健康影响。虽然该审查没有提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露数据,但它强调了认识各种呼吸道病毒感染的长期症状的重要性。这些发现是 CIDRAP 为呼吸系统疾病提供基于证据的见解所做努力的一部分,但没有明确提及风险水平或有针对性的应对行动。此次审查基于现有数据,不包括新病例调查或具体预防措施。

规模、影响与风险
该审查强调了各种呼吸道感染后出现长期症状的 患病率,但证据中没有提供具体的病例数、死亡数或暴露数量。风险没有明确说明。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Lingering respiratory virus symptoms aren’t limited to COVID, new review shows - CIDRAP

News Brief
A new review by CIDRAP reveals that lingering respiratory virus symptoms are not exclusive to COVID-19 and are also common after other respiratory infections such as the flu and RSV. The report, published on September 8, 2026, emphasizes the need for broader understanding of post-infection health effects. While the review does not provide specific case counts, death numbers, or exposure data, it highlights the importance of recognizing prolonged symptoms in various respiratory viral infections. The findings are part of CIDRAP's efforts to offer evidence-based insights into respiratory illnesses, with no explicit mention of risk levels or targeted response actions. The review is based on existing data and does not include new case investigations or specific prevention measures.
Original source text (English)

独立评审员尚不能确定老年人在即将到来的季节是否需要注射第二剂 RSV - Medical Daily

Independent Reviewers Cannot Yet Say Whether Older Adults Need a Second RSV Dose This Coming Season - Medical Daily
新闻简报独立审查人员目前无法确定老年人在即将到来的季节是否需要第二剂 RSV 疫苗。该审查强调了现有 疫苗 的强有力保护,但发现了关于需要重复剂量的证据差距。这一结论是基于一项独立的证据审查,该审查没有发现关于第二剂疫苗对该人群的有效性的结论性数据。调查结果是初步的,审查正在进行中。证据中没有报告具体病例或死亡人数,也没有明确说明风险级别。该审查强调需要进一步研究以确定老年人是否需要额外剂量以维持对 RSV 的免疫力。
查看新闻五要素
时间
Sep 08, 05:38 pm EDT.
地点与对象
斯特凡诺斯·西西帕斯(Stefanos Tsitsipas)去年两天无法行走,周一击败了前十名种子。西西帕斯表示,背痛让他无法行走,正在考虑退休。
事件
独立审查人员无法确定老年人是否需要在本季节接种第二剂 RSV。该审查确定了 疫苗 的强大保护作用,但指出有关重复剂量的证据存在差距。
规模、影响与风险
该审查的重点是老年人是否需要注射第二剂 RSV,但没有报告具体病例或死亡人数。证据中没有明确说明风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
审查正在进行中,调查结果是初步的。证据有限,关于第二剂的必要性尚未得出明确的结论。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Independent Reviewers Cannot Yet Say Whether Older Adults Need a Second RSV Dose This Coming Season - Medical Daily

News Brief
Independent reviewers are currently unable to determine whether older adults need a second dose of the RSV vaccine this coming season. The review highlighted strong protection from the existing vaccine but identified a gap in evidence regarding the need for repeat doses. This conclusion is based on an independent evidence review, which found no conclusive data on the effectiveness of a second dose in this demographic. The findings are preliminary, and the review is ongoing. No specific case or death counts were reported in the evidence, and the risk level was not explicitly stated. The review underscores the need for further research to establish whether additional doses are necessary for older adults to maintain immunity against RSV.

建议部分儿童接种第二剂 RSV - Medscape

Second RSV Dose Recommended for Some Kids - Medscape
新闻简报美国儿科学会 (AAP) 扩大了 RSV 免疫接种的建议范围,以涵盖更多进入 RSV 第二季的儿童,特别是那些患有某些高危疾病的儿童。更新后的指南强调,患有先天性心脏病、肺部或神经肌肉疾病、唐氏综合症的 8 至 19 个月的儿童,或在没有持续医疗支持的情况下早产(妊娠 32 周之前)的儿童,无论母亲是否接种过疫苗或之前的婴儿是否接种过 疫苗接种,都应接受第二剂疫苗。AAP 的建议中还包括患有囊性纤维化、慢性肺病的儿童以及所有美洲原住民和阿拉斯加原住民儿童。这些变化基于的证据表明,即使在第一个季节之后,一些儿童仍然面临着患严重 RSV 疾病的较高风险。AAP 强调,第一季的保护仍然是首要任务,更新后的指南反映了 RSV 传播和严重程度不断变化的模式。美国妇产科医师学会 (ACOG) 将孕产妇 RSV 疫苗 窗口期延长至 3 月 1 日,与 AAP 的建议保持一致。建议临床医生向家人传达这些最新信息,因为它们基于最新的证据和公共卫生观察。
查看新闻五要素
时间
美国儿科学会扩大了针对 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的免疫接种建议,以包括更多进入第二个 RSV 季节的儿童,理由是有证据表明某些医疗状况继续使幼儿面临更高的严重疾病风险。AAP 的指导强调在第一个 RSV 季节期间保护婴儿。这些变化反映出越来越多的证据表明,尽管一岁后因呼吸道合胞病毒住院的可能性通常会下降,但一些儿童的风险仍然较高。
地点与对象
这些建议适用于进入第二个 RSV 季节的 8 个月至 19 个月的儿童,这些儿童患有先天性心脏病、某些肺部或神经肌肉疾病,或者患有唐氏综合症或某些与 RSV 风险较高相关的染色体疾病,以及妊娠 32 周之前出生且不需要持续药物或支持的儿童。AAP 随附的技术报告发现,大多数因 RSV 住院的婴儿没有潜在的高危健康状况。
事件
AAP 主席、医学博士、博士安德鲁·拉辛 (Andrew Racine) 在宣布这些建议的新闻发布会上表示,高危儿童“无论其母亲在怀孕时是否接种过疫苗,也无论婴儿在第一个 RSV 季节是否接受过 RSV 免疫”,都应接受第二剂疫苗。
规模、影响与风险
在明尼苏达大学传染病研究与政策中心 疫苗 诚信项目进行的独立证据审查中,尼塞韦单抗 将住院风险降低了 93%。
应对、状态与不确定性
扩大建议之际,其他主要医疗组织更新了 2026-2027 年呼吸道病毒季节指南,其中一些变化反映了 RSV 传播时间的变化。美国妇产科医师学会 (ACOG) 将孕产妇 RSV 疫苗 ABRYSVO 的推荐窗口期从 9 月 1 日延长至 3 月 1 日,而不是在 1 月 31 日结束。临床医生应向家属强调,RSV 模式的变化和不断变化的证据加强了医疗组织之间关于第二剂疫苗益处的共识。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Second RSV Dose Recommended for Some Kids - Medscape

News Brief
The American Academy of Pediatrics (AAP) has expanded its recommendations for RSV immunization to include more children entering their second RSV season, particularly those with certain high-risk medical conditions. The updated guidance highlights that children aged 8 to 19 months with congenital heart disease, lung or neuromuscular conditions, Down syndrome, or those born prematurely (before 32 weeks of gestation) without ongoing medical support should receive a second dose regardless of maternal immunization or prior infant vaccination. The AAP also includes children with cystic fibrosis, chronic lung diseases, and all Native American and Alaska Native children in the recommendations. These changes are based on evidence showing that some children remain at a higher risk for severe RSV disease even after their first season. The AAP emphasized that first-season protection remains a priority, and the updated guidance reflects evolving patterns in RSV circulation and severity. The American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists (ACOG) extended the maternal RSV vaccine window to March 1, aligning with the AAP’s recommendations. Clinicians are advised to communicate these updates to families, as they are grounded in the latest evidence and public health observations.
Original source text (English)
Jump to Key Points The American Academy of Pediatrics expanded its recommendations for immunization against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) to include more children entering their second RSV season, citing evidence that certain medical conditions continue to put young children at a higher risk for severe disease. The expansion includes children aged 8 months through 19 months entering their second RSV season who have congenital heart disease, certain lung or neuromuscular conditions, or have Down syndrome or certain chromosomal conditions associated with a higher RSV risk, and those born before 32 weeks of gestation who do not need ongoing medication or support. The updated guidance for second-season immunization also includes children with certain forms of cystic fibrosis, chronic lung diseases requiring recent medical support, and all Native American and Alaska Native children. High-risk children should receive a second dose “regardless of whether their mother was immunized when they were pregnant and regardless of whether or not that infant received an RSV immunization during their first RSV season,” said Andrew Racine, MD, PhD, president of the AAP, during a press briefing announcing the recommendations. The changes reflect growing evidence that although the likelihood of hospitalization for RSV generally declines after the first year of life, some children remain at a substantially higher risk . SUGGESTED FOR YOU Children with chronic conditions affecting multiple respiratory, cardiovascular, or gastrointestinal organs had more than twice the rate of hospitalizations during their second RSV season than during their first season, according to the AAP’s accompanying technical report. For infants entering their first RSV season, the AAP recommends a monoclonal antibody unless the mother received an RSV vaccine during pregnancy. RSV Season Is Lasting Longer The expanded recommendations come as other major medical organizations have updated their guidance for the 2026-2027 respiratory virus season, with some changes reflecting shifts in the timing of RSV circulation. The American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists (ACOG) extended the recommended window for the maternal RSV vaccine ABRYSVO from September 1 through March 1, rather than ending on January 31. “We made this change because the RSV disease season is now occurring later in the spring, and it is also more severe than it used to be,” said Christina Davidson, MD, chief medical officer at ACOG. “The extension of the eligibility window will help provide pregnant patients and their infants with additional protection.” Pregnant people should receive the vaccine between 32 and 36 weeks of gestation, provided delivery is not expected within 2 weeks. If a patient does not receive the vaccine, or if the infant is born within 14 days of vaccination, the newborn should receive a monoclonal antibody for RSV. First-Season Protection Remains the Priority Despite the expansion of second-season recommendations, the AAP’s guidance emphasized protecting infants during their first RSV season. Nirsevimab reduced hospitalization risk by as much as 93% in an independent evidence review conducted by the Vaccine Integrity Project, part of the University of Minnesota’s Center for Infectious Disease Research and Policy. The AAP’s accompanying technical report found that most infants hospitalized with RSV had no underlying high-risk medical condition. Clinicians should emphasize to families that changes in RSV patterns and evolving evidence reinforce the consensus among medical organizations on the benefits of a second dose, said Sarah Nosal, MD, president of the American Academy of Family Physicians. “As federal guidance evolves, the science behind vaccines remains strong,” Nosal said. The second-dose RSV recommendations are “grounded in the latest evidence, informed by public health experts, and shaped by what family physicians are seeing every day in their patients,” Nosal said. Lara Salahi is a health journalist based in Boston. 新的审查显示,挥之不去的呼吸道病毒症状不仅限于新冠病毒

Lingering respiratory virus symptoms aren’t limited to COVID, new review shows
新闻简报《呼吸学》杂志上发表的一项新的系统综述检查了非新冠肺炎呼吸道感染后的持续症状,包括流感和 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV)。该综述分析了 2004 年 1 月至 2025 年 8 月期间涉及 631,863 人的 13 项观察性研究,强调肺功能障碍、疲劳和其他症状可能在感染后持续存在。对于 RSV,心血管并发症是最常见的持续性病毒感染后症状,16% 的住院成人在感染后 29 天至 6 个月内出现心脏相关事件。23% 的流感患者和 55% 的人类偏肺病毒患者也报告有持续症状。作者警告说,大多数研究都是观察性的,并且集中于住院患者,因此很难知道较轻微的感染后持续出现症状的频率。他们强调需要标准化的定义和结果测量,以更好地定义疾病负担并为有针对性的后续行动和预防策略提供信息。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该综述分析了2004年1月至2025年8月期间的研究,具体发现来自2009年、2017年以及持续至2025年的数据。发表日期为2026-09-08。
地点与对象
这些研究的参与者来自美国、澳大利亚、德国和新加坡,涉及住院患者和普通人群。
事件
《呼吸学》上发表的一篇系统综述检查了非新冠肺炎呼吸道感染(包括流感和呼吸道合胞病毒)后的持续症状。研究发现,感染后肺功能障碍、疲劳和其他症状可能会持续存在,其中心血管并发症是 RSV 感染后最常见的。
规模、影响与风险
该综述包括13项观察性研究,涉及631,863名参与者。对于呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV),16%的住院成人患者在感染后29天至六个月期间出现心脏相关事件,2.1%出现新发心脏疾病。流感患者的持续症状报告率为23%,而人类副流感病毒患者的持续症状报告率为55%。
应对、状态与不确定性
该审查强调需要标准化定义和结果衡量标准,以更好地定义疾病负担。大多数研究都是观察性的,主要针对住院患者,因此很难评估较轻病例的症状。研究结果基于现有数据并提出了未来的研究方向。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Lingering respiratory virus symptoms aren’t limited to COVID, new review shows

News Brief
A new systemic review published in Respirology examined persistent symptoms following non-COVID respiratory infections, including influenza and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). The review analyzed 13 observational studies involving 631,863 people from January 2004 to August 2025, highlighting that lung dysfunction, fatigue, and other symptoms can linger after infection. For RSV, cardiovascular complications were the most common persistent post-viral complaint, with 16% of hospitalized adults experiencing heart-related events within 29 days to six months post-infection. Persistent symptoms were also reported in 23% of flu patients and 55% of those with human metapneumovirus. The authors caution that most studies were observational and focused on hospitalized patients, making it difficult to know how often lingering symptoms occur after milder infections. They emphasize the need for standardized definitions and outcome measures to better define disease burden and inform targeted follow-up and prevention strategies.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Main navigation News Antimicrobial Resistance COVID-19 Chronic Wasting Disease Influenza Public Health Alerts All Topics A-Z Coronavirus Vaccines Roadmap Influenza Vaccines Roadmap Roadmaps for Priority Pathogens Vaccine Integrity Project CIDRAP Leadership Forum . . Topics & Projects Antimicrobial Stewardship Chronic Wasting Disease Osterholm Update Podcasts Newsletters About CIDRAP CIDRAP in the News Our Staff Our Director Osterholm in the Press Shop Merchandise Contact Us About Search CLOSE Search Support Menu Lingering respiratory virus symptoms aren’t limited to COVID, new review shows Laine Bergeson COVID-19 Influenza, General Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) PonyWang / iStock Share Copied to clipboard For many people who contract COVID-19, the initial infection comes with a set of worrisome symptoms, from muscle aches and fatigue to trouble breathing—and for a subset of those people, symptoms persist beyond the acute infection phase. Long COVID has become a well-known post-viral condition that can take a significant toll on health and quality of life. But SARS-CoV-2 isn’t the only virus that can have lingering aftereffects. Other respiratory viral infections (RVIs), which affect millions of people every year, can trigger persistent physical, cognitive, and psychiatric symptoms. A new systemic review published in Respirology examined the size and scope of persistent symptoms following non-COVID respiratory infections like influenza and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). “Understanding post-acute sequelae of non–COVID-19 RVIs, given their global burden and relative undercharacterisation, is important to guide clinical care and public health policy,” write the researchers, who were led by a team from Duke-NUS Medical School in Singapore. Lung problems common after flu For the review, researchers analyzed 13 observational studies involving 631,863 people from January 2004 to August 2025. The flu was the most studied infection, and lung dysfunction was the most frequently reported persistent symptom. In a small 2009 study (48 participants), 54% of participants had abnormal pulmonary function 11 to 13 months after acute influenza infection. Roughly one-third reported persistent respiratory symptoms one year after infection, and over 40% reported reduced exercise tolerance. In another small study (47 participants), this one from 2017, 78% of people who survived severe flu infection had reduced ability to transfer oxygen from their lungs into their bloodstream two years later. Understanding post-acute sequelae of non–COVID-19 RVIs, given their global burden and relative undercharacterisation, is important to guide clinical care and public health policy. Findings from larger studies mirrored those from the smaller studies. Among 951 Australians diagnosed as having influenza, 23% reported symptoms after 12 weeks, and 4.4% had moderate-to-severe difficulty carrying out everyday activities. A US study of 20,844 hospitalized patients found that one in 10 still had a cough or shortness of breath six months after infection. An analysis of German insurance data from nearly 570,000 people found that 23% reported memory problems, and 44% had a distorted sense of taste or a reduced sense of smell three to six months after acute illness. Heart-related symptoms most common after RSV Two of the 13 studies in the review looked at RSV and found that cardiovascular complications were the most common persistent post-viral complaint. Among 441 US adults hospitalized with RSV, 16% experienced a heart-related event from 29 days to six months after acute infection, making the monthly rate of cardiovascular events 1.6 times higher during the post-infection period than during the six months before hospitalization. A cohort study of 1,553 adults with RSV in Singapore found that 2.1% experienced new-onset cardiac disorders after infection. The review included one small study of human metapneumovirus. Among 92 hospitalized adults, 55% reported at least one persistent symptom three to nine months after infection. Shortness of breath was most common (29%), followed by fatigue (17%), and cough (17%). The authors caution that most studies included in the review were observational and focused on hospitalized patients, making it difficult to know how often lingering symptoms occur after milder infections. They also note differences in outcome measures and follow-up approaches between the studies, concluding that future studies should use standardized definitions and outcome measures “to better define disease burden and inform targeted follow-up and prevention strategies.” Related news Previous Next Vaccine recommendations and access, plus measles deaths: 3 questions, zero consensus: The State of US Vaccine Policy Izzy Brandstetter Figueroa, PhD, MPH, Riley Mulholland, MPH, Jess Steier, DrPH Vaccine recommendations and access, plus measles deaths: 3 questions, zero consensus: The State of US Vaccine Policy Izzy Brandstetter Figueroa, PhD, MPH, Riley Mulholland, MPH, Jess Steier, DrPH COVID-19 may trigger the same immune pathway as lupus Laine Bergeson CIDRAP Op-Ed: ‘Died with, not from’ was a real COVID problem. Time
The review analyzed studies from January 2004 to August 2025, with specific findings from 2009, 2017, and ongoing data up to 2025. The publication date is 2026-09-08.
Location and population
The studies included participants from the US, Australia, Germany, and Singapore, involving hospitalized patients and general populations.
Event
A systemic review published in Respirology examined persistent symptoms following non-COVID respiratory infections, including influenza and RSV. It found that lung dysfunction, fatigue, and other symptoms can linger after infection, with cardiovascular complications being most common after RSV.
Scale, impact and risk
The review included 13 observational studies with 631,863 participants. For RSV, 16% of hospitalized adults experienced heart-related events within 29 days to six months post-infection, and 2.1% had new-onset cardiac disorders. Persistent symptoms were reported in 23% of flu patients and 55% of those with human metapneumovirus.
Response, status and uncertainty
The review highlights the need for standardized definitions and outcome measures to better define disease burden. Most studies were observational and focused on hospitalized patients, making it difficult to assess symptoms in milder cases. The findings are based on existing data and suggest future research directions.

独立审查支持 2026-27 季节呼吸道病毒免疫接种 - 患者护理在线

Independent Review Supports Respiratory Virus Immunizations for 2026-27 Season - Patient Care Online
新闻简报Patient Care Online 于 2026 年 9 月 4 日发表的文章重点介绍了一项支持 2026-27 季节呼吸道病毒免疫接种的独立审查。正如 Cochrane 评价中指出的那样,该评价证实了 RSV 疫苗 在高危人群中的安全性和有效性。虽然证据没有具体说明确切的病例或死亡人数,但它强调了 FDA 对这些 疫苗 的批准及其降低 传播 和严重程度的潜力。文章没有提供有关调查、检测或应对措施的详细信息,但强调了呼吸道病毒免疫在公共卫生规划中的重要性。重点是一般性建议以及对 疫苗 开发和分发的更广泛影响,而没有提及受影响的具体地点或人群。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章于2026-09-04发表。事件日期在证据中未明确说明,但内容提及的是2026-27赛季。
地点与对象
证据没有具体说明受呼吸道病毒免疫影响的特定地点或人群。重点是 2026-27 赛季的一般建议和批准。
事件
一项独立审查支持 2026-27 赛季的呼吸道病毒免疫接种。该事件由 Patient Care Online 报道,并根据提供的证据予以确认。
规模、影响与风险
证据没有提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。它只提到了疫苗的批准及其在高危人群中的安全性和有效性,没有明确的风险声明。
应对、状态与不确定性
证据表明,独立审查已确认 RSV 疫苗 的安全性和有效性。不过,没有提供有关调查、测试或应对措施的具体细节。

Independent Review Supports Respiratory Virus Immunizations for 2026-27 Season - Patient Care Online

News Brief
The article published on September 4, 2026, by Patient Care Online highlights an independent review that supports respiratory virus immunizations for the 2026-27 season. The review confirms the safety and efficacy of RSV vaccines across high-risk groups, as noted in the Cochrane Review. While the evidence does not specify exact case or death counts, it emphasizes the approval of these vaccines by the FDA and their potential to reduce transmission and severity. The article does not provide detailed information about the investigation, testing, or response measures, but it underscores the importance of respiratory virus immunizations in public health planning. The focus is on general recommendations and the broader implications for vaccine development and distribution, without mentioning specific locations or populations affected.

Abrysvo and Arexvy Market Report Analyzes Industry Trends, Key Growth Factors, and Future Perspectives - openPR.com

News Brief
The news report published on openPR.com on 2026-09-04 discusses the market trends and growth factors for RSV vaccines, specifically highlighting the approval of ABRYSVO by the FDA for preventing lower respiratory tract disease in adults aged 18-59 and pregnant individuals between 32 and 36 weeks of gestation. The report emphasizes the increasing demand for RSV vaccines, driven by an aging population, the significant burden of respiratory infections, and heightened public health awareness. It also outlines the market's segmentation, including clinical indications, age groups, and end users, to provide a structured understanding of the target populations. The report notes that ABRYSVO is the only RSV vaccine authorized for use in pregnant individuals to protect infants from birth through six months. However, it does not provide specific case or death counts, nor does it mention any explicit risk levels. The information is based on market analysis rather than public health data, and the report does not detail current investigation or response status. The report is a market analysis and does not constitute an outbreak or health event report.

Abrysvo and Arexvy Market Report Analyzes Industry Trends, Key Growth Factors, and Future Perspectives Understanding the Future Outlook of the Abrysvo or Arexvy Market The Abrysvo or Arexvy market is gaining attention due to its potential to address significant respiratory health challenges. With growing awareness and advances in vaccine technology, this sector is poised for substantial expansion. Below, we explore the market's historical growth, driving forces, key players, current trends, and segment classifications to provide a comprehensive perspective. Download a free sample of the abrysvo or arexvy market report: https://thebusinessresearchcompany.com/sample_request?id=81935210&type=smp&name=Abrysvo%20Or%20Arexvy%20Market%20Report%202026&utm_source=OpenPR&utm_medium=Paid&utm_campaign=Sep_PR Market Value Expectations and Growth Factors in the Abrysvo or Arexvy Market During the past years, the market's expansion has been largely driven by an unmet demand for RSV vaccines, the increasing elderly population, the significant burden of respiratory infections, regulatory approvals, and heightened public health awareness. Looking ahead, growth is expected to continue, fueled by the aging demographic, rising rates of RSV-related hospitalizations, broader vaccination guidelines, improved vaccine access through healthcare providers, and a stronger emphasis on preventive healthcare. Key trends shaping the forecast period include a growing commitment to RSV prevention, increased vaccination rates among older adults, expansion of adult immunization initiatives, vaccine administration in hospitals and communities, and targeted preventive care for high-risk groups. Prominent Organizations Leading the Abrysvo or Arexvy Market The competitive landscape of the Abrysvo or Arexvy market is primarily shaped by major pharmaceutical companies such as Pfizer Inc. and GlaxoSmithKline plc (GSK). These industry leaders are at the forefront of developing and distributing RSV vaccines to meet evolving healthcare needs. View the full abrysvo or arexvy market report: https://thebusinessresearchcompany.com/report/abrysvo-or-arexvy-global-market-report?utm_source=OpenPR&utm_medium=Paid&utm_campaign=Sep_PR Emerging Innovations and Developments in the Abrysvo or Arexvy Market Market frontrunners are concentrating efforts on creating advanced vaccine formulations, including bivalent prefusion F (RSVpreF) vaccines, aimed at enhancing protection, improving safety profiles, and targeting vulnerable populations. Bivalent RSVpreF vaccines provide immunity against both main RSV subtypes (RSV-A and RSV-B) by focusing on the prefusion state of the fusion protein, a critical target for neutralizing antibodies. For example, in October 2024, Pfizer Inc., a US-based pharmaceutical company, secured FDA approval for ABRYSVO, a bivalent RSV prefusion F vaccine, to prevent lower respiratory tract disease in adults aged 18 to 59 at increased risk. This approval also expanded the vaccine's indication to the broadest adult population. Additionally, ABRYSVO remains the only RSV vaccine authorized for use in pregnant individuals between 32 and 36 weeks of gestation to protect infants from birth through six months. Detailed Segmentation of the Abrysvo or Arexvy Market The report categorizes the Abrysvo or Arexvy market according to the following segments: 1) Clinical Indication: Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) Prevention and Infant Protection 2) Age Group: Adults aged 18-59, Older Adults aged 60 and above, and Pregnant Individuals in the 32-36 weeks gestational period 3) End User: Healthcare Providers, Pregnant Individuals, and High-Risk Adults This segmentation provides a structured understanding of the market's scope and target populations, essential for tailoring healthcare strategies and vaccine deployment. 秋季 疫苗:随着一些病例的增加,最新的 Covid、流感 RSV 疫苗 建议 - NBC 5 芝加哥

Fall vaccines: Latest Covid, flu RSV vaccine recommendations as some cases rise - NBC 5 Chicago

Fall vaccines: Latest Covid, flu RSV vaccine recommendations as some cases rise - NBC 5 Chicago

News Brief
The article, published on September 3, 2026, by NBC 5 Chicago, discusses the latest fall vaccine recommendations for flu, COVID-19, and RSV in the U.S. It highlights that medical groups like the American Academy of Pediatrics and the Infectious Diseases Society of America are urging people to get updated vaccines, especially for high-risk groups such as older adults, pregnant women, young children, and those with chronic health conditions. The CDC has not issued new guidance, but doctors are advised to follow last year’s recommendations. The FDA has approved updated flu and COVID-19 vaccines, with the latter targeting the XFG variant. Flu vaccines are recommended for nearly everyone starting at age 6 months, while RSV is noted as a growing concern during winter. The article emphasizes that vaccines reduce severe outcomes, with flu shots decreasing hospitalizations in older adults by 31% and RSV being a preventable risk. It also mentions that vaccine availability is widespread, with no evidence of increased risk for pregnant women or others receiving the vaccines.

By NBC Chicago Staff and The Associated Press • Published September 3, 2026 • Updated on September 3, 2026 at 11:54 am Log in or create a free profile to save articles BOOKMARKER A Walgreens sign advertising free flu shots is seen outside a store in Chicago, Illinois, United States, on August 9, 2026. (Photo by Marcin Golba/NurPhoto via Getty Images) Leading U.S. medical groups are urging people to get updated flu and COVID-19 vaccinations this fall, banding together Wednesday to issue shot guidelines — and highlight the science behind them — saying they hope to ease confusion caused by the Trump administration’s changing advice. Flu vaccinations are beginning and COVID-19 shots are starting to arrive after the Food and Drug Administration approved this year’s updated versions last week. Winter also brings another vaccine-preventable risk called RSV. Stream NBC 5 for free, 24/7, wherever you are. WATCH HERE In Illinois, the latest data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention shows that COVID cases are already trending upwards. The trend is the same for 47 other states, with the CDC saying the respiratory virus is "likely growing." But who should get one or all of these vaccines, and when? That advice usually comes from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, with help from its independent advisers. But despite no new scientific evidence, Trump health officials last year began pulling back on some longstanding recommendations for fall virus protection and routine children’s vaccinations. And that advisory panel, which influences state and insurance vaccine policies, is caught in a court battle. The CDC didn’t respond to questions about any new fall guidance but suggested on its website that doctors follow last year’s flu recommendations. Doctors’ groups are filling the gap: the American Academy of Pediatrics, American Academy of Family Physicians, American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists, and the Infectious Diseases Society of America each released vaccination guidelines for their patient populations. Local Frankfort 15 minutes ago Frankfort mom accused of killing son said she intended to kill other kids, husband: Pros. chicago concerts 51 minutes ago Stevie Wonder's ‘Songs in the Key of Life' tour features Chicago show “There’s been a lot of confusion and chaos regarding vaccine recommendations,” said Dr. Sandra Fryhofer, of the American Medical Association. All the groups’ recommendations plus evidence on shot safety and effectiveness are being posted on websites so “the public has clear, consistent, science-based guidance even while the federal process is in flux,” Fryhofer said. Who should get the flu shot? Just about everybody starting at age 6 months needs flu vaccination, the medical societies agree. But flu is particularly dangerous for people 65 and older, pregnant women, young children and people of any age who have chronic health problems, including asthma, diabetes, heart disease and weak immune systems. The last two flu seasons were especially harsh, with unusually high numbers of child deaths — the vast majority of them in unvaccinated kids. The vaccines don’t block all flu infections but they do help prevent the worst outcomes, decreasing older adults’ hospitalizations by 31%, according to the Vaccine Integrity Project, a public health group based at the University of Minnesota. Its analysis was published Wednesday in the medical journal JAMA. Three “enhanced” flu vaccines are designed to better protect seniors. The American Academy of Family Physicians urges them to choose one — “but do not delay your vaccination if that one’s not available,” said AAFP’s Dr. Sarah Nosal. There’s a new choice this year for people ages 50 and older, the first flu vaccine made with the same mRNA technology that was key to ending the COVID-19 pandemic. One study found Moderna’s mFlusiva was 27% more effective against symptomatic illness among 50- to 64-year-olds than a standard shot. The FDA just approved the new option and the company expects some doses to be available soon at certain pharmacies. The shot-averse could choose a nasal spray, FluMist, if they’re ages 2 to 49 and meet other health criteria. Flu shots are currently widely available at pharmacies like Walgreens and CVS. They are also offered in some doctors and pediatricians offices. Who can and should get the COVID vaccine? Earlier this month, the FDA approved Pfizer and BioNTech's updated COVID shot for 2026-27 targeting the XFG variant. COVID-19 shots once were recommended for just about everyone by federal health agencies, but like last year, the FDA has only formally approved them for higher-risk people — those ages 65 and older or younger people with at least one health condition that increases their risk. Still, the family physicians recommend all adults get a COVID-19 vaccination, especially seniors and those with other risk factors. Adults 65 and older should get a fall dose and then a second dose six months later, as immunity wanes over time, Nosal said. The American Academy of Pediatrics recommends a COVID-19 shot for all children ages 6 months to 23 months, and for older kids with high-risk conditions. It also supports vaccination for any child whose parent wishes it. With millions of shots administered, there’s no evidence that coronavirus vaccination during pregnancy increases the risk of miscarriage or other problems, “so please get the vaccine,” said ACOG’s Dr. Christina Davidson. Depending on age, people can choose a shot from Moderna, Pfizer, or the only non-mRNA version from Novavax and Sanofi. Just like with flu vaccines, these shots are better at preventing severe disease than mild cases. The Vaccine Integrity Project said last fall’s COVID-19 shots cut the risk of hospitalization by 53% in seniors and slashed emergency room visits for very young children. Common side effects for flu and COVID-19 vaccines are mild and temporary, such as sore arms, fatigue, headaches, muscle aches, and mild fever. They occurred somewhat more often with the new mFlusiva than with standard flu shots. Where you can get the COVID shot For COVID-19 shots, states have more pharmacy rules. For example, Walgreens says it offers those shots without a prescription in all but two states, Arizona and Oregon, but can provide doses for children as young as 3 in some states while others have higher age limits. Medicare covers the fall shots and most private health insurers say they plan to continue doing so through 2027. A release dated Aug. 27 said this season's COVID shots will begin shipping "immediately" and was expected to be available in pharmacies, hospitals and clinics across the US "in the coming days." When to get your vaccines October is ideal for a flu vaccine, as influenza typically starts spreading in November. COVID-19 tends to rise in late summer, as it is now, and again after the winter holidays. Fryhofer says it’s fine to time the coronavirus shot a few weeks ahead of an event you wouldn’t want to miss because of illness, like a special trip or get-together. What about the RSV vaccine? RSV, or respiratory syncytial virus, typically is a cold-like nuisance during the fall, winter and early spring. But it can be severe, even life-threatening, for infants and older adults. The family physicians recommend a one-time vaccination for adults 75 and older, people 50 to 74 who are at increased risk, or younger people with weak immune systems. It’s also recommended during late pregnancy to protect infants born during RSV season. Separately, the pediatricians recommend a vaccine-like medication for those infants whose moms weren’t vaccinated and some other high-risk babies. Column: Belief in time-tested vaccines needs shot in the arm - Chicago Tribune

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Column: Belief in time-tested vaccines needs shot in the arm - Chicago Tribune

News Brief
The article, published on September 4, 2026, by the Chicago Tribune, discusses the resurgence of measles in Illinois, particularly in Douglas County, which has seen four confirmed cases despite a high vaccination rate among school-age children. The piece highlights the CDC's report of nearly 3,000 measles cases nationwide, the largest in a decade, with 94% of cases linked to unvaccinated individuals. It attributes this trend to the anti-vaccine stance of Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr., who has questioned vaccine safety and influenced national policy. Illinois officials support the AAP’s vaccination schedule but note the state ranks 27th in vaccination rates. The article emphasizes the importance of vaccines in preventing diseases and calls for maintaining current research and safety policies.
Original source text (English)
Ephrem Chen, 9, receives a COVID-19 booster shot and a flu vaccine from an IDPH nurse during a walk-in clinic on Dec. 3, 2023, at St. Procopius School in Pilsen. Illinois is recommending the COVID-19 vaccine this respiratory virus season for all children ages 6 to 23 months old and for children between the ages of 2 and 17 if they are in higher risk groups, or if their parents want them to have the shot, recommendations that are similar to those made by the American Academy of Pediatrics. (Brian Cassella/Chicago Tribune) By Charles Selle | For the Lake County News-Sun PUBLISHED: September 4, 2026 at 2:29 PM CDT Getting your Trinity Audio player ready... Some parents must have been on the fence when it came to ensuring their children were vaccinated before beginning new-school-year classes last month. At least in Douglas County along the Interstate 57 corridor. That’s one conclusion coming from the measles outbreak in the east-central Illinois county which is home to the burgs of Tuscola and Arcola. Four confirmed cases of the disease have been reported in the county by the state’s Department of Public Health despite some 98% of school-age children there being vaccinated. If that’s the case, it seems our vaccination policies need a shot in the arm to get to the 100% mark. Or we need to start getting straight talk about vaccines from the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services. Most folks understand that vaccines are modern medical miracles. With immunizations, parents no longer have to be concerned about their children contracting childhood diseases such as polio, tetanus and chickenpox. Smallpox was a feared cause of death in the U.S. Since 1977, it has been declared eliminated. The World Health Organization estimates vaccines prevent around 3.5 million to 5 million deaths per year across the globe. Despite those in some quarters questioning the use of vaccines during the COVID-19 pandemic, serums manufactured and distributed during the outbreak saved millions of Americans and more at a time when millions were dying after being infected with the coronavirus. Highly contagious measles was considered eradicated in the U.S. in 2000. But increasing doubts about vaccines and U.S. Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr.’s insistence that shots are unsafe has led to an uptick in cases of measles across the nation. Health officials in Illinois and other states continue to preach vaccine safety, especially when it comes to measles and other diseases considered in the childhood category. Yet, this year there have been nine cases of measles in Illinois, including one in Kane County, according to a recent Chicago Tribune story. Last year, the state had 14 cases of measles. The U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention said there have so far been nearly 3,000 confirmed cases of measles in the nation, the largest number in a decade. Of those cases, some 94% were in unvaccinated people, according to the CDC. Nationally, Utah has had 526 cases this year, and South Carolina has had 670, the CDC reports. Illinois has a high rate of vaccinations among its populace, state officials say. Yet, we remain in 27th place among all states, just below New Mexico and just above North Carolina, according to one health study. Vermont is the highest-vaxxed state in the nation; Mississippi the worst. Illinois requires all school-age children to be immunized against a bunch of diseases — among them diphtheria, whooping cough, tetanus, polio, rubella, mumps and measles — before enrolling in school. State health officials support the American Academy of Pediatrics’ vaccination schedule. Meanwhile, the Chicago-based American Medical Association continues to urge adults, especially seniors, to be vaccinated for a variety of ailments. Our immune systems weaken over time, medical professionals say, and keeping up to date with vaccines becomes even more important as we age. Considered necessary immunizations by the CDC include seasonal flu shots, current COVID-19 vaccines and shingles jabs. Also, vaccines for RSV, a respiratory disease, pneumonia, tetanus boosters and Hepatitis B infections. Health Secretary Kennedy, 72, a lawyer and determined anti-vaccine advocate, has questioned the efficacy of a number of those immunizations and has attempted to strong-arm changes to national vaccine policy, according to several reports. He has challenged the safety of shots for common diseases, like measles, contended COVID-19 serums were dangerous, and theorized that many vaccines have not been properly tested and can cause a range of dangerous side effects. During his term, he has ended development of mRNA vaccines, withdrawn funding for an international vaccine alliance, and narrowed access to COVID-19 shots. His views run counter to medical professionals and scientists who maintain the secretary seeds fear among Americans when it comes to the safety of immunizations. Especially parents. Millions and millions of Americans have received a litany of immunizations over decades of medical care. The facts are that these diseases have been stamped out through the use of vaccines. Diseases which once shortened the lifespans of Americans continue to be afterthoughts. There’s no reason to change research policies. Solid science backs the outcomes of vaccines. Charles Selle is a former News-Sun reporter, political editor, and editor. sellenews@gmail.com. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

由于 CDC 对 疫苗 指南保持沉默,医生领导的团体为即将到来的季节制定了自己的指南 - 佐治亚州公共广播

With the CDC silent on vaccine guidance, physician-led groups make their own for the upcoming season - Georgia Public Broadcasting
新闻简报这篇文章讨论了佐治亚州的情况,由于 CDC 尚未提供更新的指导,由医生领导的团体针对即将到来的呼吸道病毒季节发布了自己的 疫苗 建议。在此之前,疾病预防控制中心免疫实践咨询委员会(ACIP)进行了重组,该委员会因法院案件而无法开会或发布指导。这些建议包括针对婴儿的一次性 RSV 疫苗 剂量(除非母亲在怀孕期间接种疫苗)以及针对大多数人的年度更新的 COVID 疫苗。CDC 延长了其 2025 年建议,佐治亚州公共卫生部支持医疗团体的方法,强调共同的临床决策。文章还指出,佐治亚州学龄儿童的 疫苗接种 比率仍低于大流行前的水平,但豁免人数正在增加。RSV 和流感被认为是对老年人和儿童更为严重的病毒,流感 疫苗 在预防严重疾病方面的有效率为 40-60%。Local public health departments in Georgia offer 疫苗 for adults and children regardless of insurance status.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于 2026 年 9 月 3 日,讨论了即将到来的呼吸道病毒季节,该季节通常从秋季持续到仲春,其中冬季是高峰期。
地点与对象
该报告重点关注佐治亚州,当地公共卫生部门为成人和儿童提供 疫苗,无论其保险状况如何,并提到了一般人群,包括婴儿、老年人和免疫功能低下的个人。
事件
由于 CDC 尚未提供最新指南,包括美国儿科学会、美国家庭医师学会和美国妇产科学院在内的医生领导的团体已针对即将到来的呼吸道病毒季节发布了自己的 疫苗 建议。这是 CDC 免疫实践咨询委员会 (ACIP) 重组以来的第二年。
规模、影响与风险
文章指出,据报道,佐治亚州学龄儿童的 疫苗接种 比率仍低于大流行前的水平,并且 疫苗 豁免正在增加。它还强调了 RSV 对老年人和儿童的潜在严重性,流感 疫苗 在预防严重疾病方面的有效率为 40-60%。RSV 可引起严重的并发症,特别是对于婴儿和免疫功能低下的个体。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 延长了针对即将到来的呼吸道病毒季节的 2025 年建议。医疗团体与 疫苗 诚信项目合作发布了建议,这些建议附带在《美国医学会杂志》上发表的证据审查。由于法院尚未就委员会改革的合法性做出裁决,CDC 的 ACIP 目前无法发布指导。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

With the CDC silent on vaccine guidance, physician-led groups make their own for the upcoming season - Georgia Public Broadcasting

News Brief
The article discusses the situation in Georgia where physician-led groups have issued their own vaccine recommendations for the upcoming respiratory virus season, as the CDC has not provided updated guidance. This follows the restructuring of the CDC's Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices (ACIP), which has been unable to meet or issue guidance due to a court case. The recommendations include a one-time RSV vaccine dose for infants, unless mothers are vaccinated during pregnancy, and an annual updated COVID vaccine for most people. The CDC has extended its 2025 recommendations, and the Georgia Department of Public Health supports the medical groups' approach, emphasizing shared clinical decision-making. The article also notes that vaccination rates among school-aged children in Georgia remain below pre-pandemic levels, with exemptions on the rise. RSV and flu are highlighted as viruses that can be more severe in older adults and children, with the flu vaccine being 40-60% effective in preventing serious illness. Local public health departments in Georgia offer vaccines for adults and children regardless of insurance status.
Original source text (English)

GPB’s Sofi Gratas has more. Credit: Adobe Stock A group of independent medical associations is issuing its own vaccine recommendations ahead of respiratory virus season, apart from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. Respiratory virus season usually lasts from fall through mid-Spring, with cases of common infectious diseases spiking in the winter. It’s the second year since the overhaul of the CDC’s Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices that guidelines from the American Academy of Pediatrics, American College of Family Physicians and American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists have been released independent from the federal government. The groups are in collaboration with the Vaccine Integrity Project housed at the University of Minnesota. In years past, the groups were aligned with the federal agency, said Dr. Bruce Gellin of the Vaccine Integrity Project on a press call Tuesday. “Unfortunately, today, the federal government is not fulfilling that responsibility in the way that historically has,” he said. “But the need for that work has not disappeared.” Last year, the ACIP indicated it intended to stray from the traditionally recommended vaccine schedule and instead encourage “shared clinical decision-making" for several childhood vaccines. Members also considered removing a universal recommendation that newborns get vaccinated against Hepatitis B. But now, ahead of the expected spike in flu, COVID, and RSV, the ACIP can’t meet or issue guidance on vaccines until the court decides if President Trump’s overhaul of the committee was illegal. RELATED: Federal judge pauses changes made by the CDC's vaccine advisory committee The CDC extended its 2025 recommendations for the upcoming respiratory virus season this week. Reported vaccination rates this year among school-aged children remain below pre-pandemic levels, with the rate of vaccine exemptions filed in Georgia schools on a steady uptick. Georgia’s Department of Public Health is supportive of the recommendations from medical groups and those made by the CDC “for many years,” confirmed agency spokesperson Nancy Nydam Shirek. It also supports the key principle of “shared clinical decision-making.” “DPH continues to urge parents to consult with their healthcare provider to determine what best meets their child’s medical needs and to ask questions they may have about vaccines and the diseases they prevent,” Nydam Shirek wrote. Local public health departments across the state offer vaccines for adults and children, regardless of insurance status. Guidance important for months to follow All recommendations from the participating organizations, in collaboration with the Vaccine Integrity Project, come with an evidence review, each published in JAMA, Dr. Ronald Nahass with the Infectious Diseases Society of America told the press. “I think the point is to make sure that the medical community, which we're here representing that, is uniform in our effort to bring clarity and to restore that trust,” Nahass said. Last fall and winter, Georgia saw a five-year high for flu deaths with over 200 reported, and an extended RSV season. Both viruses can be more serious in older adults and kids. The flu vaccine is 40-60% effective in preventing serious illness that could result in hospitalization. It’s widely recommended for everyone over six months old. A vaccine to protect against respiratory syncytial virus, or RSV, is at this point recommended as a one-time dose. Symptoms mimic the common cold but can cause severe complications in older adults and immunocompromised people. Babies, too, can be hospitalized with RSV, particularly if it morphs into a lower respiratory infection, said Dr. Robert Wiskind, a practicing physician and representative for the Georgia chapter of the American Academy of Pediatrics. He recommends infants get a dose in their first year, unless mothers are vaccinated while pregnant. That's in line with the national organization's guidelines this year. “It’s been remarkably successful,” he said. “And it's safe, and it is only one dose that lasts throughout the five to six-month respiratory season.” The AAP also recommends an annual, updated COVID vaccine for almost everyone, even as spikes in viral infection have been less severe. “It is true that a lot of people who get COVID now have a milder case, probably because they have immunity from previous illnesses or previous vaccination,” Wiskind said. “But it's also true that COVID-19 can still be a significant illness for adults, for children, especially young children or children with other health conditions.” Last year, access to COVID vaccines for many was thrown into question when the federal Food and Drug Administration placed restrictions on who was eligible for the updated vaccines, under the direction of Health and Human Services Secretary Robert F. Clover 发布了其联合呼吸 疫苗 在老年人中的第 2 期积极数据 - Dealroom

Clover posts positive Phase 2 data for its combination respiratory vaccines in older adults - Dealroom
新闻简报Clover Biopharmaceuticals 于 2026 年 9 月 6 日报告了两种实验性联合呼吸系统 疫苗 的 2 期试验的积极初步结果。该试验在澳大利亚进行,受试者为 420 名 60 至 85 岁的成年人,测试了针对 RSV (呼吸道合胞病毒) 和 hMPV(人类偏肺病毒)的 疫苗,第三种候选药物还包括针对 PIV3(副流感病毒)的保护类型 3)。两种 疫苗 都触发了强烈的 免疫应答,针对 RSV 的抗体增加了 6 至 9 倍,针对 hMPV 的抗体增加了 6 至 8 倍,并且最年长的参与者(75 岁以上)没有下降。在 RSV 暴发 期间,接种疫苗组的呼吸道感染比安慰剂组减少了约 62%。疫苗 的耐受性良好,没有严重的副作用。Clover 正在推进这两种候选药物并寻求全球合作伙伴,并引用数据和成功的制造来降低进入更大规模试验的风险。这些调查结果是初步的,需要进一步调查。
查看新闻五要素
时间
第 2 期试验结果于 2026 年 9 月 6 日报告。该试验的实施期间包括当地 RSV 暴发,免疫应答 在 疫苗接种 后 28 天进行测量。
地点与对象
该试验在澳大利亚进行,涉及 420 名 60 至 85 岁的成年人,专门针对呼吸道感染风险较高的老年人。
事件
Clover Biopharmaceuticals 报告了两种实验性联合呼吸道 疫苗 的 2 期试验的积极初步结果,该试验旨在保护老年人免受 RSV 和其他相关呼吸道病毒的侵害。该结果得到了该公司的确认,并且是其多剂量呼吸 疫苗 市场竞争战略的一部分。
规模、影响与风险
两种 疫苗 都触发了强烈的 免疫应答,针对 RSV 的病毒抗体增加了 6 至 9 倍,针对 hMPV 的病毒抗体增加了 6 至 8 倍。在 75 岁以上的参与者中,没有观察到 免疫应答 显着下降。在 RSV 暴发 期间,接种疫苗组的呼吸道感染比安慰剂组减少了约 62%。疫苗 的耐受性良好,没有报告严重的副作用。
应对、状态与不确定性
Clover 正在推进 疫苗 候选人并寻求全球合作伙伴。这些数据与成功的制造相结合,降低了进入更大规模后期试验的风险。这些调查结果是初步的,需要进一步调查。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Clover posts positive Phase 2 data for its combination respiratory vaccines in older adults - Dealroom

News Brief
Clover Biopharmaceuticals reported positive preliminary results from a Phase 2 trial of two experimental combination respiratory vaccines on 6 September 2026. The trial, conducted in Australia with 420 adults aged 60 to 85, tested vaccines targeting RSV (respiratory syncytial virus) and hMPV (human metapneumovirus), with a third candidate also including protection against PIV3 (parainfluenza type 3). Both vaccines triggered strong immune responses, with a 6-to-9-fold rise in antibodies against RSV and 6-to-8-fold against hMPV, and no decline in the oldest participants (75+). During an RSV outbreak, vaccinated groups saw about 62% fewer respiratory infections than placebo. The vaccines were well tolerated with no serious side effects. Clover is advancing both candidates and seeking global partners, citing the data and successful manufacturing as reducing the risk of moving into larger trials. The findings are preliminary and require further investigation.
Original source text (English)
Clover Biopharmaceuticals reported positive preliminary results on 6 September 2026 from a mid-stage (Phase 2) trial of two experimental vaccines designed to protect older adults against several respiratory viruses at once. The results matter because they push Clover closer to competing in the fast-growing market for shots that guard against multiple winter respiratory infections in one dose. The trial, run in Australia in 420 adults aged 60 to 85, tested two candidates: SCB-1022, which targets RSV (respiratory syncytial virus, a common cause of serious lung infections in the elderly) and hMPV (human metapneumovirus, a related respiratory bug), and SCB-1033, which adds protection against a third virus, PIV3 (parainfluenza type 3). Both are protein-based vaccines built on Clover's Trimer-Tag platform, the company's own technology for assembling vaccine ingredients. Twenty-eight days after vaccination, both shots triggered strong immune responses — roughly a 6-to-9-fold rise in virus-fighting antibodies against RSV and 6-to-8-fold against hMPV — with no fall-off in the oldest participants (75+). Adding the third virus target did not weaken the response to the other two. Vaccinated groups also saw about 62% fewer respiratory infections than placebo during a local RSV outbreak, and the shots were generally well tolerated with no vaccine-related serious side effects. Clover is positioning the vaccines partly as a booster for people who have already had an approved RSV shot, and says the data — plus a successful commercial-scale manufacturing run — lowers the risk of moving into larger late-stage trials. The company plans to advance both candidates and seek global partners. Source: Clover Biopharmaceuticals, 6 September 2026
View five news elements
Time
The Phase 2 trial results were reported on 6 September 2026. The trial was conducted over a period that included a local RSV outbreak, with immune responses measured 28 days after vaccination.
Location and population
The trial was conducted in Australia, involving 420 adults aged 60 to 85, specifically targeting older adults who are at higher risk for respiratory infections.
Event
Clover Biopharmaceuticals reported positive preliminary results from a Phase 2 trial of two experimental combination respiratory vaccines designed to protect older adults against RSV and other related respiratory viruses. The results were confirmed by the company and are part of its strategy to compete in the market for multi-dose respiratory vaccines.
Scale, impact and risk
Both vaccines triggered strong immune responses, with a 6-to-9-fold rise in virus-fighting antibodies against RSV and 6-to-8-fold against hMPV. No significant decline in immune response was observed in participants aged 75+. During an RSV outbreak, vaccinated groups saw about 62% fewer respiratory infections than placebo. The vaccines were well tolerated with no serious side effects reported.
Response, status and uncertainty
Clover is advancing both vaccine candidates and seeking global partners. The data, combined with successful manufacturing, lowers the risk of moving into larger late-stage trials. The findings are preliminary and require further investigation.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

中国批准全球首个针对婴儿 RSV 的九个月预防性抗体 - Insights.citeline.com

China Approves Global-First Nine-Month Prophylactic Antibody For Infant RSV - insights.citeline.com
新闻简报中国批准了全球首个针对新生儿和婴儿的呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)九个月预防性抗体,标志着儿科医疗保健的重大进步。这种药物 retavibart 由 Trinomab Pharmaceutical 开发,针对 RSV 的预融合蛋白,旨在在婴儿的第一个 RSV 季节提供保护。该批准得到了国家药品监督管理局 (NMPA) 的确认,适用于足月儿、晚期早产儿以及感染高风险人群。证据强调,尽管没有提供具体的病例数或死亡人数,但在高危人群中观察到了更高的疗效。响应状态为活跃,该药物现已在中国上市。这一进展可能会重塑针对弱势婴儿的 RSV 预防策略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
China approved the nine-month prophylactic antibody for infant RSV on Sep 04 2026, with the event date being the first RSV season for newborns and infants.
地点与对象
中国,针对新生儿和婴儿,包括足月儿和晚期早产儿,以及RSV感染高危人群。
事件
中国国家药品监督管理局 (NMPA) 批准了世界上第一个用于预防 RSV 的单克隆抗体,可为进入第一个 RSV 季节的新生儿和婴儿提供九个月的保护。这是基于官方批准的确认事件。
规模、影响与风险
该药物瑞塔维巴特 (retavibart) 可为 RSV 感染高风险婴儿提供九个月的保护,在这些人群中显示出更大的疗效。所提供的证据中没有报告具体的病例数、死亡数或暴露数量。
应对、状态与不确定性
该药已获批准,现已在中国上市。响应状态为活动状态,证据中没有未解决的信息或限制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

China Approves Global-First Nine-Month Prophylactic Antibody For Infant RSV - insights.citeline.com

News Brief
China has approved the world’s first nine-month prophylactic antibody for respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) in newborns and infants, marking a significant advancement in pediatric healthcare. The drug, retavibart, developed by Trinomab Pharmaceutical, targets the prefusion protein of RSV and is designed to protect infants during their first RSV season. This approval is confirmed by the National Medical Products Administration (NMPA) and is intended for full-term and late preterm infants, as well as those at high risk of infection. The evidence highlights that greater efficacy was observed in high-risk groups, though no specific case counts or death numbers are provided. The response status is active, with the drug now available for use in China. This development could reshape RSV prevention strategies for vulnerable infants.
Original source text (English)

Chinese approval for world-first homegrown antibody for RSV (Shutterstock) China’s National Medical Products Administration (NMPA) has granted the world’s first approval for a monoclonal antibody for respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) prophylaxis that provides nine-month protection for newborns and infants, including full-term and late preterm infants and those at high risk of RSV infection, who are entering or born during the first RSV season. Key Takeaways China has approved the world’s first prophylactic antibody to provide nine-month protection for newborns and infants entering or born during their first respiratory syncytial virus season - Trinomab's retavibart. The drug retavibart targets the prefusion protein of RSV and was developed by Zhuhai-headquartered Trinomab Pharmaceutical. B routine priorityPublic-health news

HOMETOWN HEALTH: Family medicine physician outlines recommendations for flu, RSV and COVID-19 - WDBJ7

News Brief
The article outlines vaccine recommendations for flu, RSV, and COVID-19 from a family medicine physician, emphasizing the importance of vaccination to protect individuals and communities. Published on September 4, 2026, in Roanoke, Virginia, the recommendations are based on an independent review by the American Medical Association and the University of Minnesota’s Vaccine Integrity Project, which was published in the medical journal JAMA. These findings are guiding several U.S. medical groups, including the American Academy of Family Physicians, in providing clear guidance. The ongoing impact of respiratory viruses, including flu, RSV, and COVID-19, is noted as causing hundreds of thousands of hospitalizations and tens of thousands of deaths annually. The recommendations include annual flu shots for everyone six months and older, with special considerations for pregnant individuals and adults 65 and older. For RSV, adults 75 and older and those at higher risk between 50 and 74 years are advised to receive a one-time vaccine, while high-risk children up to 19 months may receive monoclonal antibodies. The article highlights the need for vaccination to prevent severe illness and reduce the spread of infection.

(WDBJ) - An independent fall vaccine review from the American Medical Association and the University of Minnesota’s Vaccine Integrity Project was published Wednesday in the medical journal JAMA. It is helping to guide recommendations from several U.S. medical groups, including the American Academy of Family Physicians. Dr. Sarah Nosal, president of the American Academy of Family Physicians, said the goal is to provide clear guidance. “We’re gonna bring you, bring you the strongest, safest recommendations to keep your family healthy, just like you’re prioritizing,” Nosal said. Flu vaccine recommendations Beginning in October, everyone six months and older is advised to get an annual flu shot. People who are pregnant should also receive the flu vaccine, but should avoid the nasal spray version. Adults 65 and older should receive an enhanced vaccine, if it’s available. Adults 50 and older are approved to get the new mRNA-based flu vaccine. RSV vaccine recommendations For RSV, now through early fall, adults 75 and older, and those ages 50 to 74 at higher risk of severe illness, should receive a one-time RSV vaccine. The RSV vaccine is also recommended for people who are immunocompromised, those 32 to 36 weeks pregnant between September 1 and March 1, and infants under eight months who didn’t receive maternal protection. Children up to 19 months of age at high risk of severe RSV disease are advised to receive one of two FDA-approved monoclonal antibodies for protection. COVID-19 vaccine recommendations It is currently recommended that all adults 19 years and older who haven’t had a recent COVID-19 infection receive an updated COVID-19 vaccine. This also applies to infants between six and 23 months and children in certain high-risk groups. “This is the potential and ability to both protect ourselves from severe illness and protect the people around us from spreading infection,” Nosal said. Ongoing impact of respiratory viruses The Vaccine Integrity Project says the burden of respiratory viruses has changed since the COVID-19 pandemic. Influenza, RSV and COVID-19 continue to send hundreds of thousands of people to U.S. hospitals and lead to tens of thousands of deaths every year. Copyright 2026 WDBJ. All rights reserved. Location and population
(WDBJ) - An independent fall vaccine review from the American Medical Association and the University of Minnesota’s Vaccine Integrity Project was published Wednesday in the medical journal JAMA.
Event
A family medicine physician outlined recommendations for flu, RSV, and COVID-19 vaccines, with the goal of providing clear guidance to keep families healthy.
Scale, impact and risk
The ongoing impact of respiratory viruses includes sending hundreds of thousands of people to U.S. hospitals and leading to tens of thousands of deaths annually.
Response, status and uncertainty
The recommendations are based on an independent fall vaccine review from the American Medical Association and the University of Minnesota’s Vaccine Integrity Project, published in JAMA. These are guiding several U.S. medical groups, including the American Academy of Family Physicians.
